      Not used
       Not used
      Security
   Provision
Security shall be provided by the Contractor in accordance with Item 14.
   Not used
   No injunction
The Contractor must not take any steps, including the seeking of an injunction or other order of any court, to injunct or otherwise restrain: 
the issuer of any Security from paying the Principal pursuant to that Security;
the Principal from taking any steps for the purpose of making a demand under any such Security or receiving payment under any such Security; or
the Principal using the money received under any Security.
    Reduction and release
Upon the issue of the Certificate of Practical Completion in respect of a Stage, the Principal's entitlement to Security (other than in Item 14(e)) shall be reduced by the percentage or amount in Item 14(f) as applicable, and the reduction shall be released and returned within 14 days to the other party.
The Principal’s entitlement to Security in Item 14(e) shall cease 14 days after incorporation into the Works of the plant and materials for which that Security was provided.
The Principal's entitlement otherwise to Security shall cease 14 days after Final Certificate.
Upon a party’s entitlement to Security ceasing, that party shall release and return forthwith the Security to the other party.
    Trusts and interest
Except where held by a government department or agency or a municipal, public or statutory authority, any portion of Security (and interest earned thereon) which is cash, shall be held in trust for the party providing them until the Principal or the Contractor is entitled to receive them.
Interest earned on Security not required to be held in trust shall belong to the party holding that Security.
    Replacement of Security
If:
the Contract Sum (Stage) is increased by more than 10% due to a Variation, the Contractor must within 14 days of a request to do so by the Principal provide a replacement Security; or
otherwise the Security provided is due to expire,
the Contractor must provide a replacement Security no later than 28 days prior to the date the Security expires, for the period of 1 year from the expiry of the replaced Security.
    Deed of guarantee, undertaking and substitution
Where:
a party is a related or subsidiary corporation (as defined in the applicable corporations law of the jurisdiction); and
a form of Deed of Guarantee, Undertaking and Substitution was included in the tender documents,
that party shall, within 14 days after receiving a written request from the other party, provide such Deed of Guarantee, Undertaking and Substitution duly executed and enforceable.
        Due Diligence Phase
    Proactive Review of Preliminary Design and Site 
The Contractor acknowledges that:
the Principal provided the Contractor with the Preliminary Design for each Stage prior to the Date of the Contract; and
the Principal will provide the Contractor with access to the Site for each Stage for the purposes of Due Diligence in accordance with subclause 6.8.
The Contractor must undertake a proactive and comprehensive due diligence of each Preliminary Design and each Site by physically inspecting each Site to: 
ensure that each Preliminary Design is free from Buildability Issues and for that purpose identify and propose all relevant Buildability Input in accordance with subclause 6.2; 
identify and propose all relevant Innovation Input and Risk Reduction Input in accordance with subclauses 6.5 and 6.6; and
review, discuss and agree all input proposed under subparagraphs (i) and (ii) with the Superintendent provided that, if the Contractor is unable to obtain the Superintendent’s agreement to any Due Diligence Input, then the Superintendent may give the Contractor a Direction as to how the Due Diligence Input will be resolved.
    Buildability Input
Without limiting paragraph (b), but for the purposes of paragraph (b)(i), the Contractor must:  
promptly (and in any event within 2 business days) after having been given access to each Site, commence the Due Diligence;
if any Preliminary Design contains any Buildability Issues, provide the Principal (in the draft Due Diligence Report under clause 6.3(a)(i)) with all comments, advice and other assistance necessary and appropriate to enable the Superintendent to consider how the relevant Preliminary Design should be finalised so that the Buildability Issues are fully addressed ("Buildability Input");
without limiting subparagraph (ii), provide the Superintendent with detailed advice as to the actions required to finalise the relevant Buildability Input so that:
all Buildability Issues are fully addressed; and
all Buildability Input is provided, considered and (if applicable) incorporated in the relevant final Due Diligence Report,
at a rate of progress so that the Contractor can comply with its obligations under the Contract and each Delivery Phase Approval can be achieved by each Date for Delivery Phase Approval; 
if the Contractor requires any information, guidance, or other Direction from the Superintendent for the purpose of finalising the relevant Due Diligence Report, promptly request the information, guidance or Direction from the Superintendent; 
proactively program and manage the Due Diligence Activities and finalisation of the relevant Due Diligence Report so as to ensure that there are no delays to achieving each Delivery Phase Completion or each Practical Completion of the Works and that value for money for the Principal is otherwise maximised; and
otherwise do all things necessary to ensure that each Preliminary Design is free from Buildability Issues and each final Due Diligence Report incorporates all required Buildability Input.
The Contractor:
unequivocally accepts responsibility for all work, cost, delay and disruption arising out of or in connection with Buildability Issues; and 
agrees that the Principal will not be liable upon any Claim (to the extent permitted by law) arising out of or in connection with Buildability Issues, all of which the Contractor will be deemed to have fully allowed for in the Contract Sum. 
Without limiting paragraph (b), the Contractor acknowledges that in the Delivery Phase:
the Superintendent may give the Contractor a Direction as to how to overcome a Buildability Issue, including relevant design details showing any changes to the workmanship or materials to be used to complete the Works; and
the Contractor will, to the extent permitted by law, have no Claim against the Principal arising out of or in any way in connection with: 
the Buildability Issue; 
the Superintendent's Direction; or 
any work, cost, delay or disruption arising out of or in any way in connection with the Superintendent's Direction.
    Due Diligence Report
The Contractor must:
within 14 days of being provided access to each Site - submit a draft Due Diligence Report to the Superintendent, containing all proposed Due Diligence Input;
within 7 days of being given a direction by the Superintendent under paragraph (b)(iii) agreeing to, or otherwise stating how to resolve, any Due Diligence Input - submit a revised draft Due Diligence Report to the Superintendent; and
no later than 7 days before each Date for Delivery Phase Approval - submit the final draft Due Diligence Report to the Superintendent.
If the first draft Due Diligence Report is approved by the Superintendent, then the Superintendent will issue a written notice to the Contractor.
    Risk of Buildability Issues
No representation has been, is or will be deemed to be, made to the Contractor by the Principal, the Superintendent or any of their employees, agents or consultants about the accuracy, completeness or sufficiency of the Preliminary Design or that it represents a completed design of the Works which: 
is suitable for construction purposes; or
is free from Buildability Issues.
The Principal has not assumed, nor does it assume, a duty of care to the Contractor concerning the Preliminary Design or any Buildability Issues. 
The risk of:  
Buildability Issues; and 
any delay, disruption, cost, loss or damages suffered or incurred as a result of Buildability Issues,  
is assumed by the Contractor.  
The Contractor warrants that:  
in preparing and submitting each Due Diligence Phase Fee and each Delivery Phase Price it has provided sufficient resources to: 
undertake the Due Diligence; and
provide the Superintendent with all Buildability Input and other assistance necessary to enable each Due Diligence Report to be finalised so that it captures all Buildability Issues required to enable the Contractor to finalise each Preliminary Design in the Delivery Phase so that it is free from Buildability Issues; 
the Delivery Phase Price covers, in addition to the Contractor's other obligations under the Contract, the assumption of the risks described in this subclause 6.4 including the requirement to perform all WUC arising out of or in any way in connection with overcoming all Buildability Issues; and 
without limiting paragraph (c), all work which it is required to carry out arising out of or in connection with overcoming Buildability Issues will be deemed to be part of the WUC, even if the work is not contained, described or referred to (either expressly or impliedly) in the Preliminary Design. 
    Cost Planning 
The Contractor must review the Cost Plan for each Stage, as it undertakes the Due Diligence and prepares the relevant draft Due Diligence Report, and:
ensure that the cost of construction of the Works as designed having regard to each Preliminary Design and each Site:
is in accordance with each Cost Plan and in any event does not exceed each Delivery Phase Price, except to the extent that it demonstrates in the relevant Cost Plan to the satisfaction of the Superintendent (acting reasonably) that an adjustment is required in a Provisional Allowance (Cost); and
fully takes into account all Project Objectives;
provide proactive and innovative recommendations to the Superintendent (as part of its Innovation Input) as to how each Preliminary Design should be modified to ensure that: 
the cost of the construction of the Works as designed is in accordance with each Cost Plan and does not in any event exceed the relevant Delivery Phase Price as a result of a proposed adjustment to a Provisional Allowance (Cost) referred to in paragraph (a)(i)(A); and 
the Project Objectives are maximised, 
as a result of any Due Diligence Input identified in its relevant draft Due Diligence Report without affecting compliance with the relevant Preliminary Design (other than Buildability Issues) and the other requirements of the Contract;
provide proactive and innovative recommendations to the Superintendent (as part of its Risk Reduction Input) as to any risk reduction studies the Contractor considers should be conducted at the Site, to ensure that: 
the cost of the construction of the Works as designed is in accordance with each Cost Plan and does not in any event exceed the relevant Delivery Phase Price as a result of a proposed adjustment to a Provisional Allowance (Cost) referred to in paragraph (a)(i)(A); and 
the Project Objectives are maximised, 
as a result of any risks identified in the relevant draft Due Diligence Report without affecting compliance with the relevant Preliminary Design (other than Buildability Issues) and the other requirements of the Contract;
review, discuss and agree (subject to clause 6.1(b)(iii)) all Innovation Input and Risk Reduction Input with the Superintendent;
if required by the Superintendent under subparagraph [(iv)], undertake any risk reduction studies on terms agreed by the Superintendent; and
update each Cost Plan to ensure that it accurately reflect the requirements of subparagraph (i) and the Innovation Input provided under subparagraph (ii) and agreed to be implemented under subparagraph (iv) and the outcome of any risk reduction studies undertaken under subparagraph (v).
The Contractor must:  
submit updated Cost Plans under subparagraph (a)(v) to the Superintendent;  
revise each Cost Plan submitted under subparagraph (i) in accordance with the Superintendent's directions; and 
obtain the Superintendent's approval to each updated Cost Plan as a condition precedent to payment of the relevant Due Diligence Fee. 
Each draft of each Cost Plan to be prepared under paragraph (b) must include:
identification in detail of all work required for, and all risks which could be encountered in, Practical Completion of the Works, which a prudent, competent and experienced contractor would anticipate and provide for in its cost plan for the WUC; 
a cost analysis of each component of the Works, including a detailed break-up by reference to each trade package and other component (including self-performed work, on site overhead, off-site overhead, profit and contingency on a fully open book basis, identified as a separate line item in each draft Cost Plan; 
all proposed changes to any draft Cost Plan and any Delivery Phase Price (if any) arising out of any Buildability Issues and all available measures for proactively avoiding or mitigating the effect of the Buildability Issues (including all Innovation Input under paragraph (a)(ii) and the outcomes of any Risk Reduction Input under paragraph (a)(v)), provided that no proposed Delivery Phase Price can be greater than the relevant initial  Delivery Phase Price (without the prior written approval of the Superintendent before the submission of each draft Cost Plan); and 
all such other matters as the Superintendent may require in writing.  
The Contractor must, if a draft Cost Plan submitted by the Contractor are rejected by the Superintendent (in the Superintendent's absolute discretion), submit an amended Cost Plan.
If the relevant Cost Plan is approved by the Superintendent, then the Superintendent will issue a written notice to the Contractor.
    Programming 
The Contractor must review each Program, as it undertakes the Due Diligence and prepares the relevant draft Due Diligence Report, and:  
ensure that the duration for the construction of the Works as designed having regard to each Preliminary Design and each Site: 
does not exceed the Date for Practical Completion, except to the extent that it demonstrates in the relevant Program to the satisfaction of the Superintendent (acting reasonably) that an adjustment is required in a Provisional Allowance (Time);
complies with the Master Program; and
fully takes into account all Project Objectives;
provide proactive and innovative recommendations to the Superintendent (as part of its Innovation Input) as to how the design of the Works should be modified to ensure that:
the duration of the construction of the Works as designed does not exceed any Date for Practical Completion as a result of a proposed adjustment to a Provisional Allowance (Time) referred to in paragraph (a)(i)(A); and 
the Project Objectives are maximised, 
as a result of any Due Diligence Input identified in the relevant Due Diligence Report without affecting compliance with the relevant Preliminary Design (other than Buildability Issues) and the other requirements of the Contract;
provide proactive and innovative recommendations to the Superintendent (as part of its Risk Reduction Input) as to any risk reduction studies the Contractor considers should be conducted at the Site, to ensure that: 
the duration of the construction of the Works as designed does not exceed any Date for Practical Completion as a result of a proposed adjustment to a Provisional Allowance (Time) referred to in paragraph (a)(i)(A); and 
the Project Objectives are maximised, 
as a result of any risks identified in each draft Due Diligence Report without affecting compliance with the relevant Preliminary Design (other than Buildability Issues) and the other requirements of the Contract;
review, discuss and agree (subject to clause 6.1(b)(iii)) all Innovation Input and Risk Reduction Input with the Superintendent;
if required by the Superintendent under subparagraph (iv), undertake any risk reduction studies on terms agreed by the Superintendent; and
update each Program to ensure that it accurately reflects the requirements of subparagraph (i) and the Innovation Input provided under subparagraph (ii) and agreed to be implemented under subparagraph (iv) and the outcomes of any risk reduction studies undertaken under subparagraph (v).
The Contractor must:  
submit updated Programs under subparagraph (a)(iv) to the Superintendent; 
revise each Program submitted under subparagraph (i) in accordance with the Superintendent's directions; and 
obtain the Superintendent's approval to each updated Program as a condition precedent to payment of the Due Diligence Fee. 
Each draft of each Program to be prepared under subparagraph (b)(ii) must:  
include identification in detail of all work required for, and all risks which could be encountered in, Completion of the Works, which a prudent competent and experienced contractor would anticipate and provide for in its program for the WUC; 
comply with the Master Program;
include a detailed recommendation as to every reasonably possible dates which the Principal could set as Dates for Practical Completion for the Works;
have regard to all relevant considerations including:  
the desirability of staging and sequencing the Works; and
the paramount importance to the Principal of balancing between minimising the time to achieve Completion of the Works (on the one hand) and maximising time certainty for the Principal (on the other hand); 
all proposed changes to each draft Program and each Date for Practical Completion (if any) arising out of any Buildability Issues and all available measures for proactively avoiding or mitigating the effect of the Buildability Issues (including all Innovation Input under paragraph (a)(ii) and the outcomes of any Risk Reduction Input undertaken under subparagraph (a)(v)), provided that no proposed date for completion can be after the relevant Date for Practical Completion (without the prior written approval of the Superintendent before the submission of the relevant draft Program); and
include all such other matters as the Superintendent may require in writing.  
The Contractor must:  
if a draft Program submitted by the Contractor under paragraph (b) is rejected by the Superintendent (in the Superintendent's absolute discretion), submit an amended Program (within the time required by the Superintendent); 
in the process of preparing every Program, co-operate with the Principal, the Superintendent and all other people nominated by the Superintendent for the purpose of furthering the considerations referred to in subparagraph (c)(iv); and 
ensure that no proposed Date for Practical Completion in a draft Program exceeds the relevant initial Date for Practical Completion (without the prior written approval of the Superintendent before the submission of the draft Program). 
If the Program is approved by the Superintendent, then the Superintendent will issue a written notice to the Contractor. 
    Other Due Diligence Phase Obligations
In addition to the Contractor's obligations under this clause 6, the Contractor must do all such things or tasks as may be necessary to achieve each Delivery Phase Approval, including to provide the Superintendent with such assistance, provide such documents and attend such meetings as the Superintendent may require in writing for the purpose of obtaining each Delivery Phase Approval.
    Site Access During the Due Diligence Phase
The Principal will use its reasonable endeavours to provide the Contractor with such access to each Site during the Due Diligence Phase as may be reasonably necessary for the carrying out of the relevant Due Diligence.
The Contractor acknowledges and agrees that, without limiting paragraph (a), Site access during the Due Diligence Phase will be contingent upon:
the Principal having a right of access to the relevant Site;
evidence satisfactory to the Contract Administrator under subclause 19.1 that the Contractor has caused to be effected and maintained or otherwise have the benefit of the insurances required under the Contract;
the Contractor having submitted a request in writing to the Superintendent during the Due Diligence Phase for access to the Site setting out:
the areas of the proposed access;
the timing of the proposed access;
the reasons why the proposed access is necessary to enable the Contractor to commence and progress the Due Diligence Activities; and
the mitigation measures that the Contractor proposes to put in place to avoid any disruption or inconvenience that may be caused to the Principal, other contractors, residents and any other person authorised by the Principal or the Superintendent to occupy, use, operate, maintain or access the Site if the Contractor is given the proposed access; and
the Contractor's request being approved by the Superintendent in writing.
    Negotiations Prior to Delivery Phase Approval
Prior to each Date for Delivery Phase Approval, and by no later than the date specified by the Superintendent, the Contractor must:
notify the Superintendent and the Principal of its proposed adjustment (if any) required to:
the relevant date for commencement of Works on Site;
the Preliminary Design to constitute the Design Documents (For Construction), having regard to the Contractor's obligations between subclauses 6.1 and 6.4;
the relevant Provisional Allowance (Cost) of the relevant Delivery Phase Price; and
the relevant Date for Practical Completion, 
arising as a result of design development, cost planning and programming carried out during the relevant Due Diligence Phase including having regard to the Contractor’s obligations under this clause 6; and
thereafter undertake genuine and good faith negotiations with the Principal to reach agreement, in the Principal’s absolute discretion, as to such adjustments (if any).
As part of the process of undertaking negotiations with the Principal, the Contractor must provide such documents and information as the Superintendent may require including:
a detailed breakdown of cost in respect of each Delivery Phase Price on a fully open book basis; and
evidence of pricing from proposed subcontractors and suppliers.
If agreement on all of the matters in paragraph (a) is reached by each Date for Delivery Phase Approval, then:
the Principal will record that agreement in writing by way of formal amendment to the Annexure Part A and (if applicable) the other documents forming part of the Contract; and
the Contractor must execute the amendment referred to in subparagraph (i) on the date required by the Principal, provided that such amendment is subject to, and will only take effect upon, execution by the Principal and the relevant Delivery Phase Approval being achieved in accordance with clause 6.10.
    Delivery Phase Approval
The Principal must:
If a Delivery Phase Approval is achieved:
issue a written notice to the Contractor stating that Delivery Phase Approval is achieved; and
execute the amendment referred to in subclause 6.9(c) on the same date that the notice is issued under sub subparagraph A; and
if the relevant Delivery Phase Approval has not been achieved, issue a written notice so advising the Contractor, in which case subclause 6.11 will apply.
If the Principal issues a notice under subparagraph (a)(i)A, then:
the Contractor must:
immediately commence to carry out the remainder of the WUC including to complete the design of and construct, commission, complete and handover the Works in accordance with the terms of the Contract (as amended in accordance with subclause 6.9(c); and
unless otherwise stated, carry out the WUC at its cost; and
the Principal must give the Contractor sufficient access to the relevant Site to allow it to commence work on Site in accordance with subclause 24.1.
    Phased Engagement
The Contractor acknowledges and agrees that:
the Principal may, in its absolute discretion, issue a notice under clause 6.10(a)(ii) if a Delivery Phase Approval has not been achieved by the relevant Date for Delivery Phase Approval;
it will have no entitlement to complete design of or construct, commission, complete and handover any part of the Works during the relevant Delivery Phase unless the Principal issues a notice under subclause 6.10(a)(i)A; and
the splitting of the WUC into the Due Diligence Phase and the Delivery Phase is solely for the benefit of the Principal including, without limiting the other elements required to achieve each Delivery Phase Approval, to:
enable the Contractor to undertake the Due Diligence;
enable the Principal to be satisfied (in its absolute discretion) that it will maximise value for money for the Principal to have the Contractor proceed with the relevant Delivery Phase;
give the Principal an opportunity to elect (in its absolute discretion) not to proceed with the Contractor from the relevant Due Diligence Phase to the relevant Delivery Phase; and
to reach agreement in relation to the matters contemplated in subclause 6.9 (in its absolute discretion),
and the rights and obligations of the parties and the functions of the Superintendent are to be construed accordingly.
The Principal may, in the Principal’s absolute discretion, at any time and from time to time, by written notice to the Contractor unilaterally extend the relevant Date for Delivery Phase Approval.
Neither an extension under paragraph (b), nor the issue of a notice under subclause 6.10(a)(ii) will:
limit or affect the Contractor's obligations or liabilities under this Contract or prejudice the right of the Principal to exercise any right or remedy (including recovery of damages, whether while electing to keep the Contract on foot or after termination) which it may have where the Contractor breaches the Contract, whether under the Contract or otherwise according to law; or
entitle the Contractor to bring a Claim against the Principal.
The Contractor acknowledges that any act or omission of the Principal, which prevents a Delivery Phase Approval being achieved by the relevant Date for Delivery Phase Approval, will not:
entitle the Contractor to bring a claim against the Principal; or
limit or affect any right of the Principal or the Superintendent, including the right of the Principal to elect not to proceed with the Contractor to deliver the Works or to issue a notice under subclause 6.10(a)(ii),
whether on the basis that the obligation to finalise, agree, achieve or obtain the relevant matter has become an obligation to finalise, agree, achieve or obtain the relevant matter within a reasonable time or on any other basis at law or in equity.
The Contractor acknowledges that:
an absolute discretion in the Superintendent or the Principal under this clause 6 is not required to be exercised for the benefit of the Contractor; and
the exercise or failure to exercise such an absolute discretion is not capable of being the subject of a dispute or difference for the purpose of clause 43 or otherwise subject to review.
    Failure to Achieve Delivery Phase Approval
If the Principal issues a notice under clause 6.10(a)(ii), then:
the Contractor will:
have no entitlement to design, commence, construct, commission, complete or handover any part of the Works;
be entitled to payment of the relevant Due Diligence Phase Price due and payable for the WUC completed in accordance with the Contract before issue of the relevant notice; and
not be entitled to payment of any portion of the relevant Delivery Phase Price or to make any Claim (to the extent permitted by law) against the Principal arising out of, or in any way in connection with, the relevant notice, this Contract, the WUC or the Works other than for the amount payable under subparagraph (ii);
subject to paragraph (c), the Contractor must:
comply with subclause 8.5, including by handing over to the Superintendent copies (or, where required by the Superintendent, originals) of all Contract Documents prepared by the Contractor to the date of issue of the relevant notice (whether complete or not);
immediately take all steps necessary to novate to the Principal (or its nominee) any of the subcontractors required by the Superintendent to be novated; and
co-operate with the Principal and any third parties required by the Superintendent, including the Host Nation, and take all other steps necessary, to ensure that the Principal is able to re-tender or procure the execution of the Works under paragraph (c)(i).
the Principal may:
re-tender the execution of the Works (whether with or without obtaining a tender from the Contractor) or procure the execution of the Works in any other manner which the Principal may determine; and
without limiting the licence granted under subclause 10.2, use the Contract Documents for the purposes of re-tendering or procuring the execution of the Works under subparagraph (i).
       Service of notices 
    General
A notice, demand, certification, process or other communication relating to the Contract must be in writing in English and may be given by an agent of the sender and must be delivered by email.
    Notices by email
Subject to subclause 7.3, notice is given by email at the earlier of: 
delivery to the email address to which it was sent; or
one hour after the email enters the server of the email address to which it was sent, provided that no delivery or transmission error is received by the sender within one hour of the time of sending shown on the "sent" email. 
    After hours notices
If a notice is given:
after 5.00 pm in the place of receipt; or
on a day which is not a business day in the place of receipt,
it is taken as having been given on the next business day.
      Contract documents
    Discrepancies
The order of precedence of the Contract Documents is as set out in article 3 of the Formal Instrument of Agreement, except that in the event of any ambiguity, discrepancy or inconsistency in or between any specification or technical document comprising the Contract:
figured shall prevail over scaled dimensions in a discrepancy; and
the higher standard of quality or quantum will prevail as directed by the Superintendent.
Otherwise, if either party discovers any inconsistency, ambiguity or discrepancy in any document prepared for the purpose of carrying out WUC, that party shall give the Superintendent written notice of it. The Superintendent, thereupon, and upon otherwise becoming aware, shall direct the Contractor as to the interpretation and construction to be followed.
The Contractor shall bear the cost of compliance with a Direction under this subclause and will not be entitled to make any Claim against the Principal arising out of or in connection with the Direction or the inconsistency, ambiguity or discrepancy.
    Principal-supplied documents
The Principal shall supply to the Contractor the documents and number of copies thereof, both stated in Item 16.
They shall:
remain the Principal’s property and be returned to the Principal on written demand; and
not be used, copied nor reproduced for any purpose other than WUC.
    Contractor-supplied documents and design review
The Contractor shall submit to the Superintendent the documents and number of copies at the times or stages stated in Item 17.
Other documents and information required by the Contract, unless elsewhere stated in the Contract, shall be submitted not less than 14 days before the Work described in the documents is commenced and shall be in a form satisfactory to the Superintendent.
The Superintendent may:
review any submitted draft Design Documents (For Construction), or any resubmitted draft Design Documents (for Construction), prepared and supplied by the Contractor; and
within the number of days specified in Item 18 of the submission by the Contractor of such draft Design Documents (for Construction) or resubmitted draft Design Documents (for Construction), reject the draft Design Documents (for Construction) if in the Superintendent's reasonable opinion, the draft Design Documents (for Construction) do not comply with the requirements of the Contract.
If any draft Design Documents (for Construction) are rejected, the Contractor must submit amended draft Design Documents (for Construction) to the Superintendent.
The Contractor must not commence construction of any part of the Works to which any draft Design Documents (for Construction) it has submitted to the Superintendent applies, unless the Superintendent has had the number of days specified in the Item 18 to review the draft Design Documents (for Construction) and has not rejected the draft Design Documents (for Construction).
If the Contractor submits a document to the Superintendent, then:
the Superintendent shall not be required to check that document for errors, omissions, inconsistencies, ambiguities, discrepancies or compliance with the Contract; and
notwithstanding subclause 2.1, any Superintendent's acknowledgment or approval shall not prejudice the Contractor’s obligations;
Copies of documents supplied by the Contractor shall be the Principal’s property but shall not be used nor copied otherwise than for the use, repair, maintenance or alteration of the Works.
    Availability
The Contractor shall keep available to the Superintendent and the Principal:
on Site, one complete set of documents affecting WUC and supplied by a party or the Superintendent; and
at the place of manufacture or assembly of any significant part of WUC off Site, a set of the documents affecting that part.
    Confidential information 
The parties shall ensure that there are kept confidential such documents, samples, models, patterns and other information as are supplied and clearly identified as confidential or that a party, acting reasonably, would assume to be confidential.
If required in writing by a party, the other party shall enter into a separate agreement not to disclose to anyone else any confidential matter even after Final Certificate or earlier termination of the Contract.
The Contractor acknowledges that nothing in this clause prevents the Principal from disclosing confidential information where the disclosure: 
is disclosed by the Principal to its personnel solely in order to comply with its obligations, or to exercise its rights, under this Contract;
is disclosed to the Principal’s internal management personnel, solely to enable effective management or auditing of Contract-related activities;
is disclosed by the Principal to the responsible Minister;
is disclosed by the Principal in response to a request by a House or a Committee of the Parliament of the Commonwealth of Australia;
is shared by the Principal within the Principal's organisation, or with another agency, where this serves the Commonwealth’s legitimate interests;
is authorised or required by law to be disclosed; or
is in the public domain otherwise than due to a breach of this subclause 8.5.
Where the Principal discloses confidential information to another person pursuant to subclauses 8.5(a) to 8.5(e), the Principal will notify the receiving person that the information is confidential.
In the circumstances referred to in subclauses 8.5(a), 8.5(b) and 8.5(e) the Principal agrees not to provide the information unless the receiving person agrees to keep the information confidential.

   Media
The Contractor shall not disclose any information concerning the project for distribution through any communications media without the Principal’s prior written approval (which shall not be unreasonably withheld). The Contractor shall refer to the Principal any enquiries from any media concerning the project.
       Assignment and subcontracting
   Assignment
Neither party shall, without the other’s prior written approval (including terms) assign the Contract or any payment or any other right, benefit or interest thereunder.
    Subcontracting generally
The Contractor shall engage and retain the consultants identified in the Contractor’s tender. The Contractor shall not without the Superintendent's prior written approval (which shall not be unreasonably withheld) allow a Subcontractor to assign a subcontract or any payment or any other right, benefit or interest thereunder.
With a request for approval, the Contractor shall give the Superintendent written particulars of the Work to be subcontracted and the name and address of the proposed Subcontractor. The Contractor shall give the Superintendent other information which the Superintendent reasonably requests, including the proposed subcontract documents without prices.
Within 14 days of the Contractor’s request for approval, the Superintendent shall give the Contractor written notice of approval or of the reasons why approval is not given.
The Superintendent may give approval under this clause on such reasonable conditions as the Superintendent considers appropriate or necessary, including, without limiting the generality of the above, a condition that any subcontract include one or more of the following:
provision that the Subcontractor shall not assign nor subcontract without the Contractor’s written consent;
provisions which may be reasonably necessary to enable the Contractor to fulfil the Contractor’s obligations to the Principal; and
provision that if the Contract is terminated and upon the Subcontractor being paid the sum certified by the Superintendent as owing to the Subcontractor, the Contractor and the Subcontractor shall, after the Principal has done so, promptly execute a deed of novation in the form of Annexure Part C.
For the purpose of effecting such novation only, the Contractor hereby irrevocably appoints the Superintendent to be the Contractor’s attorney with authority to execute such documents as are necessary to give effect to the novation and to bind the Contractor accordingly and, where the Subcontractor is a consultant, provision that the Subcontractor shall effect and maintain professional indemnity insurance on the same terms as are required under Items 24(c) and 24(d).
