2. 
INTRODUCTION 
2.1. 
Invitation to Tender 
2.1.1. 
The Commonwealth of Australia (Commonwealth) as represented by the 
Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade (the Department or DFAT) invites 
interested parties (Tenderers) to submit tenders (Tenders) for the provision of Head 
Works Contractor (HWC) for the Refurbishment of the Australian High Commission 
Staff Residential Compound (SRC) Konedobu, Port Moresby, Papua New Guinea 
(PNG) (the Project) in accordance with this Request for Tender (RFT). 
2.1.2. 
An overview of the Requirements is set out in Part 2 of this RFT, and the draft 
Contract is an Attachment released with this RFT. 
2.1.3. 
This RFT comprises tender conditions, the appendixes to these tender conditions 
and the Tender Response Forms (TRF). The TRFs are to be completed by the 
Tenderer and form the Tenderer’s Tender. 
2.1.4. 
Any resultant Contract may require the supply of the Requirements to any 
Commonwealth Entity as advised from time to time. 
2.2. 
Industry Briefing  
2.2.1. 
The Department proposes to conduct an industry briefing and site visit for this RFT, 
as follows: 
a. 
an industry briefing through an online Microsoft Teams Meeting on 7th July 2026; 
and 
b. 
an in-person site visit at the Konedobu Compound, Port Moresby, PNG on 15 
July 2026. 
2.2.2. 
Attendance at the industry briefing and site visit will be limited to Registered 
Tenderers. 
2.2.3. 
The Department will notify Registered Tenderers of the details of the industry 
briefing and site visit, including:  
a. 
the scheduled date, time and meeting invite and link for the industry briefing; 
and 
b. 
the scheduled date, time, location, and logistical arrangements for the site visit. 
2.2.4. 
For the site visit, the maximum number of Tenderer personnel (whether employees, 
subcontractors, consultants or otherwise) who may attend the site visit will be a 
maximum number of three (3) representatives per Tenderer.  
2.2.5. 
Registered Tenderers should, using the Tender Registration Form at Attachment D: 
a. 
indicate if they intend to attend the in-person site visit;  
b. 
provide written details of the Tenderer; and  
c. 
provide full names, position, company, contact details, and vehicle registration 
information for all Tenderer personnel proposed to attend the site visit, which 
must not exceed the maximum number permitted under clause 2.2.4 above.  
2.2.6. 
If the Tenderer wishes to attend the industry briefing and site visit, the Tenderer 
must provide the details required under clause 2.2.5, to the Contact Email on the 
front page of this RFT by 05:00 PM Canberra Local Time (AEST), Monday, 6 July 

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 6  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
2026 (Deadline for Tenderer Registration for attendance at industry briefing and site 
visit).  
2.2.7. 
Tenderers are responsible for all arrangements and costs associated with their 
attendance at the industry briefing and the site visit as per clause 3.28 of this RFT. 
Tenderers should note that this includes costs of arranging travel, accommodation 
and visas for attending the site visit. Tenderers are also responsible for identifying 
and obtaining any visas that may be required to enter Papua New Guinea in 
sufficient time to attend any site visits held in person in Papua New Guinea. 
2.2.8. 
Attendance at the industry briefing and site visit is not mandatory, however, it is 
recommended, as it will assist potential Tenderers in better understanding the 
Requirements and in preparing submissions that address the evaluation criteria. 
2.2.9. 
The industry briefing will be recorded. Copies of the industry briefing recording, 
presentations and responses to questions provided at the industry briefing will be 
provided to Tenderers through AusTender following the industry briefing.  
2.2.10. 
Individual industry briefings and site visits will not be provided. All industry briefing 
and site visits will be conducted for the purpose of providing background information 
only. The Tenderer must not in any way rely upon the industry briefing, site visit, or 
any industry briefing materials provided, for the purposes of preparing, amending or 
negotiating its Tender or entry into any contract with the Department.  
2.2.11. 
The Tenderer is not permitted to take photographs or other electronic recordings of 
any industry briefing or site visit (including any presentation or site locations). The 
Tenderer may request photos of certain locations or aspects during the site visit. The 
Department, may at its discretion, provide photos or additional information to all 
Tenderers.  
2.2.12. 
The Department may (in its absolute discretion):  
a. 
further limit or restrict the number of Tenderer personnel; or  
b. 
exclude any or all Tenderer personnel from,   
attending the industry briefing or site visit for any reason, including if the Tenderer:  
c. 
exceeds the maximum number of Tenderer personnel specified in clause 2.2.4; 
or 
d. 
does not register their attendance as required under clause 2.2.5; or  
e. 
substitutes or replaces Tenderer personnel after the time and date specified in 
clause 2.2.6. 
2.2.13. 
Any information provided at the industry briefing and site visit will be made available 
to all Tenderers, subject to the terms and conditions of this RFT. 
2.2.14. 
Without limiting any other provision of this RFT: 
a. 
subject to the Commonwealth Procurement Rules, the Department is not 
obliged to respond to any or all questions, enquiries or other matters notified 
during the industry briefing or site visit; and  
b. 
the Department may (in its absolute discretion) publish or issue addenda under 
clause 3.1 to address any matters arising out of or in connection with the 
industry briefing and site visit. 
 

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 7  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
 
2.3. 
Project Management Contract Administrator (PMCA) 
2.3.1. 
The Department will engage a PMCA service provider to support the delivery of the 
Project. The PMCA will report to DFAT and will be responsible for the day‑to‑day 
project management, construction management, and contract administration for this 
Project. 
2.3.2. 
For the purpose of avoiding confusion, this RFT is not for the engagement of the 
PMCA service provider 
2.4. 
Contract 
2.4.1. 
The Department intends to enter into a Contract with the Successful Tenderer 
substantially in the form of the attached draft Contract. The Contract will govern the 
legal relationship between the Department and any Successful Tenderer. 
2.4.2. 
The Department proposes that any Contract arising from this RFT will be milestone 
based for the purposes of tender over approximate time period of 4.5 years 
(Practical Completion (PC) + 12 months Defects Liability Period (DLP) of the last 
residence completed). Noting that based off the tender responses and the 
milestone-based Contract, this is an estimate only. 
2.4.3. 
The Contract with the successful Tenderer (if any), will not be executed until 
Parliamentary Approval has been obtained which is expected to be tabled to 
Parliament in June/July 2026. 
2.5. 
Request for Tender  
2.5.1. 
This RFT is not a Contract between the Department and the Tenderer, nor an offer 
to Contract. Nothing in this RFT, any Tender, or any conduct or statement made 
before or after the issue of this RFT is to be construed so as to give rise to any 
contractual obligations, express or implied, or any obligations in equity. 
2.5.2. 
There is no binding agreement, express or implied between the Department and any 
Tenderer in relation to the RFT or the Requirements until a written Contract is 
executed by the Department and a Tenderer. The final Contract will contain the 
entire agreement between the Department and the Successful Tenderer.  
2.5.3. 
Clauses 2.5 does not apply to: 
a. 
the Due Diligence Information Deed Poll of Confidentiality; 
b. 
 the executed Tender Declaration in the form at Part 3 - Tenderer Response 
Schedules; and 
c. 
any other deed or contractual arrangement entered into by the Tenderer, as 
required by DFAT from time to time 
2.6. 
Governing Law 
2.6.1. 
This RFT is to be construed in accordance with, and any matter related to it is to be 
governed by, the law of the Australian Capital Territory. The courts of that Territory 
have non-exclusive jurisdiction to decide any matter related to this RFT.

3. 
CONDITIONS OF TENDER 
3.1. 
AusTender and Addenda 
3.1.1. 
This RFT is available on the AusTender website at https://www.tenders.gov.au. 
Tenderers should regularly check the AusTender website for any updates or 
addenda to this RFT. 
3.1.2. 
AusTender is the Australian Government's procurement information system. Access 
to and use of AusTender is subject to terms and conditions. In participating in this 
RFT process, Tenderers must comply with those terms and conditions and any 
applicable instructions, processes, procedures and recommendations as advised on 
AusTender at https://www.tenders.gov.au/infolinks/termsofuse.  
3.1.3. 
Tenders must be lodged on or before the Closing Time.  
3.1.4. 
The Department may extend the Closing Time in its sole discretion and will issue an 
Addendum via AusTender notifying any decision to extend. 
3.1.5. 
In the event the Department elects to vary, suspend or supplement this RFT or 
change the conditions of tender, it will issue an Addenda to the RFT posted on 
AusTender. 
3.1.6. 
Tenderers are required to log in to AusTender and collect Addenda as notified. 
3.1.7. 
The Department will accept no responsibility if a Tenderer fails to become aware of 
any Addendum notice which would have been apparent from a visit to the 
AusTender page for this RFT. 
3.1.8. 
If a Tenderer has obtained tender documentation other than from AusTender, they 
must visit AusTender, register as a user and download the tender documentation for 
this RFT. 
3.1.9. 
Upon issue, each Addendum forms part of this RFT. 
3.1.10. 
The Department will not respond to any request for clarification in relation to the RFT 
from a Tenderer received after the Deadline for Questions. 
3.1.11. 
Tenderers should acknowledge receipt of all Addenda provided in the Tenderer’s 
Response Form (TRF) 3: Addenda - Acknowledgement. 
3.2. 
Electronic Lodgement and Closing Time 
3.2.1. 
Tenders must be lodged electronically via AusTender before the Closing Time and in 
accordance with the Tender lodgement procedures set out in this RFT and on 
AusTender. 
3.2.2. 
The Closing Time will also be displayed in the relevant AusTender webpage 
together with a countdown clock that displays in real time the amount of time left 
until the Closing Time.  For the purposes of determining whether a Tender response 
has been lodged before the Closing Time, the countdown clock will be conclusive.  
For more information please see the AusTender Terms of Use. 
3.2.3. 
Where there is any inconsistency between the Tender lodgement procedures set out 
on AusTender and those set out in this RFT, this RFT will prevail. 
3.2.4. 
Tenders lodged by any other means, including by hand, facsimile or email, will not 
be considered. 

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 9  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
3.2.5. 
Tenders must remain open for acceptance (valid) for a period of at least one 
hundred and eighty (180) days from the Closing Time. 
3.2.6. 
The Department reserves the right to extend the Tender Validity Period. 
3.3. 
Access to Due Diligence Information and Designs 
3.3.1. 
Tenderers should note that Attachment C of this RFT contains a list of documents 
that comprise the design information for the Project. The design information itself is 
not included in this published RFT document as it is classified as DFAT Confidential 
Information 
3.3.2. 
For reasons of security and confidentiality, the due diligence and design information 
will not be made available to a Tenderer until the Tenderer has met the requirements 
set out in the Due Diligence Deed Poll at Attachment E to this RFT (Confidentiality 
Deed Poll).  
3.3.3. 
Tenderers must complete and return the Confidentiality Deed Poll, located at 
Attachment E of this RFT, to the Contact Email for this RFT on the front page. The 
completed Confidentiality Deed Poll must be received by DFAT in a timely manner for it 
to be processed. Any Confidentiality Deed Poll received less than 5 Business days 
ahead of the Closing Time may not be processed by the Department. 
3.3.4. 
Upon receipt of a Confidentiality Deed Poll, DFAT will review it to ensure that it has 
been correctly completed and properly executed. If DFAT determines in its sole and 
absolute discretion that the Confidentiality Deed Poll that has been submitted by a 
Tenderer does not satisfy all the requirements, DFAT may refuse to provide access 
to the due diligence or design information in Attachment C. DFAT may notify a 
Tenderer if DFAT considers that its Confidentiality Deed Poll does not comply with 
requirements, to give the Tenderer an opportunity to correct any submission errors 
or provide evidence for DFAT's consideration. This includes any Confidentiality Deed 
Poll that is incorrectly completed or improperly executed, which will be returned to 
the Tenderer for correction and resubmission.  
3.3.5. 
Any resubmitted Confidentiality Deed Poll must be received by DFAT by the date 
specified by DFAT in its correspondence returning the Confidentiality Deed Poll. Any 
Confidentiality Deed Poll received after the time specified in its correspondence may 
not be processed by DFAT. 
3.3.6. 
Once DFAT is satisfied that the Confidentiality Deed Poll has been correctly 
completed and properly executed, DFAT will email the Tenderer’s representative 
identified in the Deed access to a secure AusTender page to access and download 
the design and due diligence information. 
3.3.7. 
Tenderers that do not submit a correctly completed and properly executed 
Confidentiality Deed Poll, will not be provided access to the due diligence or design 
information in Attachment C. Any Tender submitted without reference to the 
information in Attachment C may be excluded from participation in the RFT process.  
3.3.8. 
On written notice from DFAT, or upon advice of an unsuccessful Tender outcome, 
DFAT may request that Tenderers return or destroy all DFAT Confidential 
Information including the Due Diligence Information, in accordance with the Deed 
Poll.

3.4.1. 
Before submitting an electronic Tender, Tenderers must:

3.5. 
Tender File Formats, Naming Conventions and Sizes 
3.5.1. 
Tenderers must lodge their tender in accordance with the requirements set out in 
this clause 3.5 for file format/s, naming conventions and file sizes. Failure to comply 
with any or all of these requirements may result in the Tender not uploading 
successfully and/or may eliminate the Tender from consideration. 
3.5.2. 
The Department will accept Tenders lodged that are compatible with Microsoft 
Windows 7 Enterprise or Microsoft Windows 10 (such as .doc, .docx, .xls, .xlsx or 
.PDF). 
3.5.3. 
The Tender file name/s: 
a. 
should incorporate the Tenderer’s company name; 
b.

c.

comprises multiple files; 
d.

re-name them if necessary; and 
e. 
must not exceed 100 characters including the file extension. 
3.5.4. 
Tender files:  
a. 
should be uploaded from a high-level directory on a Tenderer’s desktop, so as 
not to impede the upload process; and 
b. 
should be zipped (compressed) together for transmission to AusTender. 
3.5.5. 
If a Tender consists of multiple uploads, due to the number of files or file size, 
Tenderers must ensure that transmission of all files is completed before the Closing 
Time. 
3.5.6. 
Tenders must be completely self-contained. No hyperlinked or other material may be 
incorporated by reference.

3.6.1. 
Any attempt to lodge a Tender after the Closing Time will not be permitted by 
AusTender.

OFFICIAL
3.6.2. 
Late Tenders, incomplete Tenders, including those with electronic files that cannot 
be read or decrypted, and Tenders which the Department believes to potentially

3.7. 
Proof of Electronic Lodgement 
3.7.1. 
When a Tender lodgement has successfully completed, an official receipt is provided 
on screen. The on-screen receipt will record the time and date the Tender was 
received by AusTender and will be conclusive evidence of successful lodgement of a 
Tender. It is essential that Tenderers save and print this receipt as proof of 
lodgement. A separate email confirming receipt of the Tender will also be 
automatically dispatched to the email address of the registered user whose details 
were recorded at login. 
3.7.2. 
Failure to receive a receipt means that lodgement has not completed successfully. 
Where no receipt has been issued by AusTender, the attempted lodgement will be 
deemed to have been unsuccessful.  Tenderers should refer to AusTender for 
further instruction.

measures described on AusTender, the Department does not warrant that 
unauthorised access to information and data transmitted via the Internet will not 
occur.

communication relevant to this RFT, is not received on time, is corrupted or 
altered or otherwise is not received as sent, cannot be read or decrypted, or has 
its security or integrity compromised.

directed to: 
AusTender Help Desk

3.15. 
Statement of Tax Record and Tenderer’s Deed Poll pre-submission process 
3.15.1. 
A Tenderer may, at its discretion, up to two (2) weeks prior to the Tender Closing 
Time, submit to the Contact Email: 
a. 
a copy of their Statement of Tax Record(s) for the Tenderer (TRF1); and

OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
to seek a preliminary view by the Department, as to whether their Statement of Tax 
Record(s) and Tenderer’s Deed Poll would, if submitted by the Tenderer in their 
Tender (and otherwise in accordance with this RFT) meet the Conditions for 
Participation and Minimum Content and Format Requirement specified in clauses 
3.11 and 3.12 of this RFT. 
3.15.2. 
The Department may, in its absolute discretion, identify any apparent omissions or 
errors with the Tenderer’s Statement of Tax Record(s) and Tenderer Deed Poll. 
However, any such review or feedback is indicative only and does not constitute 
acceptance, confirmation, or waiver of any requirement. If the Department considers 
that the Tenderer’s Statement of Tax Record(s) and Tenderer Deed Poll would not 
meet the Conditions for Participation and Minimum Content and Format 
Requirement specified in clauses 3.11 and 3.12. of this RFT, the Department will 
endeavour to (but does not represent or warrant that it will) notify the Tenderer of

a preliminary view about the Tenderer’s Statement of Tax Record(s) and Tenderer 
Deed Poll, but submitting a complete and compliant final tender at the Tender 
Closing Time is ultimately the Tenderer’s sole responsibility. This includes rectifying 
any failure of the Statement of Tax Record(s) and Tenderer Deed Poll to meet the 
Conditions for Participation and Minimum Content and Format Requirements prior to 
the RFT Closing Time and the submission of its Tender.  
3.15.4. 
Regardless of any view expressed by the Department under the process in this 
clause 3.15, Tenderers are still required to comply with the Conditions of 
Participation and Minimum Content and Format Requirement specified in clauses 
3.11 and 3.12 of this RFT, which includes submission of the Statement of Tax 
Record(s) and Tenderer Deed Poll as part of the Tender. 
3.15.5. 
The Department accepts no responsibility for any view expressed by the Department 
about the Tenderer’s Statement of Tax Record(s) and Tenderer Deed Poll pursuant 
to this optional process. The Department is under no obligation to undertake any 
reviews of an early submissions and will not be liable for any failure to identify 
deficiencies. 
3.16. 
Joint Tenders 
3.16.1. 
The Department will consider a Tender from a consortium or joint venture in the form 
of a joint Tender.  
3.16.2. 
Any joint Tender must set out details of all consortia or joint venture members, 
including the role that each member would play in the delivery of the Requirements 
and must nominate a single legal entity which would, if successful, enter into a 
contract with the Department. 
3.16.3. 
Tenderers should note that agreeing to contract as a single legal entity is a 
mandatory Condition for Participation in this RFT process.  
3.17. 
Part Tenders 
3.17.1. 
Tenders offering to provide only part of the Requirements will not be considered by 
the Department. 

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 14  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
3.18. 
Alternative Tenders  
3.18.1. 
The Department may consider a Tender that does not fully conform with the 
Requirements (Alternative Tender) if: 
a. 
the Tenderer also lodges a Tender that conforms with the Requirements; and 
b. 
the Alternative Tender: 
i. 
is clearly marked as alternative; 
ii. 
proposes a solution that satisfies the functionality of the Requirements, 
including any identified essential requirements; 
iii. separately identifies in detail the proposed alternative approach and 
solution; 
iv. clearly specifies each instance of change (including the effect of the change 
on the Tenderer price); and 
v. 
clearly states the reasons for each instance of change and how the change 
is more beneficial to the Department than the approach required by the 
Requirements. 
3.18.2. 
Failure to provide the information requested in clause 3.18.1 may result in the 
Department not considering the Alternative Tender. 
3.19. 
Additional Material 
3.19.1. 
Tenderers should not provide an executive summary or other additional material 
such as supporting brochures or promotional material, unless this has been 
specifically requested in the Scope of Requirement.   
3.20. 
Conflict of Interest 
3.20.1. 
Tenderers must notify the Department as soon as practicable after becoming aware 
of an actual or potential conflict of interest, or at any time prior to receiving 
notification of the award of a contract or the termination of this RFT process. 
3.20.2. 
If the Tenderer has or may have an actual or potential conflict of interest, the 
Department may, at its discretion: 
a. 
exclude the Tender from further consideration;  
b. 
enter into discussions to seek to resolve the conflict of interest; or 
c. 
take any other action it considers appropriate. 
3.20.3. 
The Department may regard any participant in a consortium who separately Tenders 
or participates in another consortium tendering in response to the RFT as being a 
conflict of interest. 
3.21. 
Notification of Significant Events  
3.21.1. 
For the purposes of this clause, ‘Significant Event’ means: 
a. 
any adverse comments or findings made by a court, commission, tribunal or 
other statutory or professional body regarding the conduct or performance of the 
Tenderer or its officers, employees, agents or Subcontractors that impacts or 
could be reasonably perceived to impact on their professional capacity, 
capability, fitness or reputation; or 

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 15  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
b. 
any other significant matters, including the commencement of legal, regulatory 
or disciplinary action involving the Tenderer or its officers, employees, agents or 
Subcontractors, that may adversely impact on compliance with Commonwealth 
policy and legislation or the Commonwealth’s reputation. 
3.21.2. 
Until the Department determines the procurement outcome for this RFT, and during 
the time up until an agreement is executed by any Successful Tenderer, the 
Tenderer must immediately issue the Department a Notice on becoming aware of a 
Significant Event. 
3.21.3. 
The Notice issued under clause 3.21.2 must provide a summary of the Significant 
Event, including the date that it occurred and whether any of its proposed Specified 
Personnel or other personnel engaged in connection with the Goods and/or Services 
were involved. 
3.21.4. 
The Department may notify the Tenderer in writing that an event is to be considered 
a Significant Event for the purposes of this clause, and where this occurs the 
Tenderer must issue a Notice under clause 3.21.2 in relation to the event within 
three (3) Business Days of being notified by the Department. 
3.21.5. 
Where reasonably requested by the Department, the Tenderer must provide the 
Department with any additional information regarding the Significant Event within 
three (3) Business Days of the request. 
3.21.6. 
A failure by the Tenderer to comply with its obligations under this clause may result 
in the Department not considering the Tenderer’s submission.

Application of Law and Commonwealth Policy 
3.35.1. 
By submitting a Tender, Tenderers acknowledge that they have familiarised

process including: 
a. 
Division 70 of the Criminal Code Act 1995 (Cth) which makes it an offence to 
provide illegitimate benefits for foreign public officials to obtain or retain 
business; 
b. 
Division 137.1 of the Criminal Code Act 1995 (Cth) which makes it an offence to 
knowingly provide false or misleading information to a Commonwealth Entity; 
c. 
the Freedom of Information Act 1982 (Cth) which requires Australian 
Government departments and agencies to provide access to certain documents 
in their possession; 
d. 
the Auditor-General Act 1997 (Cth) which allows the Auditor-General to conduct 
a review or examination, at any time, of any aspect of the operations of 
Australian Government entities; 
e. 
the Ombudsman Act 1976 (Cth) which authorises the Ombudsman to 
investigate the administrative actions of Australian Government departments 
and agencies and to investigate the actions of certain Australian Government 
contractors; 
f. 
the Privacy Act 1988 (Cth) which aims to ensure that contractors and their 
subcontractors do not engage in an act or practice that would breach the 
Australian Privacy Principles if done by the Commonwealth. The Privacy Act 
also imposes obligations directly on contractors and subcontractors to comply 
with the Australian Privacy Principles; 
g. 
the Cybercrime Act 2001 (Cth) which criminalises certain computer related 
activities such as hacking, virus propagation and web site vandalism; 
h. 
the Automonous Sanctions Regulations 2011 (Cth). These regulations require 
anyone who holds assets or funds belonging to a person or organisation 
designated as a terrorist to freeze immediately those assets or funds. It is an 
offence to make any assets available to a terrorist. Information about current 
sanctions regimes and laws is available at: 
https://www.dfat.gov.au/international-relations/security/sanctions;  
i. 
the Work Health and Safety Act 2011 (Cth) which requires a person conducting 
or undertaking a business to ensure the health and safety of all workers and 
workplaces; 
j. 
the Government Procurement (Judicial Review) Act 2018 (Cth) which provides a 
formal complaints mechanism that applies to certain procurements;  
k. 
the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (Cth) which aims to promote the integrity 
and accountability of the Commonwealth public sector;

times for Australian small businesses, and the Payment Times Procurement

n.

3.40. 
Modern Slavery 
3.40.1. 
Under the Modern Slavery Act 2018 (Cth), the Australian Government is required to 
report annually on modern slavery risks across whole-of government procurement 
and investments. The Australian Government is committed to ensuring its supply 
chains are not linked to modern slavery and address and mitigate risks that are 
identified.  
3.40.2. 
In performing the Contract, the Successful Tenderer may be required to comply with 
one of the modern slavery model contract clause options available at 
https://modernslaveryregister.gov.au/resources/ which may require the Tenderer to: 
a. 
take reasonable steps to identify, assess and address risks of Modern Slavery 
practices in the operations and supply chains used in the provision of the Goods 
and/or Services; and 
b. 
ensure the Tenderer’s personnel responsible for managing the operations and 
supply chains used in the performance of the Contract have undertaken suitable 
training to be able to identify and report on Modern Slavery.

4. 
EVALUATION OF TENDERS 
4.1. 
Overview 
4.1.1. 
The Department will evaluate Tenders in order to identify the Tenderer which it 
considers can provide the Requirements in a manner that achieves best value for 
money for the Department.  
4.1.2. 
Value for money is a comprehensive assessment that takes into account the value 
represented by the technical assessment in the context of the risk profile presented 
by the Tenderer and its Tender, including compliance with the draft Contract and the 
cost represented by the price assessment. 
4.1.3. 
Tenderers should address each of the criteria below in the relevant TRF.  Tenderers’ 
responses in the TRFs will form the basis of the evaluation. 
4.1.4. 
The Department is not bound to accept the lowest priced or any Tender. 
4.1.5. 
In conducting the evaluation, the Department may take into account information 
provided by a Tenderer in response to one evaluation criterion in its evaluation of 
another evaluation criterion. 
4.2. 
Evaluation Criteria 
4.2.1. 
Tenders who reach Step 2 of the evaluation process will be assessed against the 
evaluation criteria detailed in clause 4.2.2. 
4.2.2. 
Evaluation Criterion  
Evaluation Criteria 
Weighting 
Technical Criteria 
 
A 
Delivery Methodology (TRF 9) 
45%

a) Understanding of the nature and scope of the Contractor’s 
Activities and the Works. 
b) Understanding and appreciation of the project 
background. 
c) Task Appreciation and Proposed Proven Methodology 
d) Understanding of the key concerns and constraints. 
e) Other information as deemed appropriate by the 
Tenderer. 
Approach to Due Diligence  
f) 
Provide specific approach to the proactive preparation, 
submission, review and development of design 
documentation and all other design tasks and activities. 
g) Ability to undertake a design Due diligence process with 
concurrent demolition activities across a rolling staged 
program of works.

commission of materials. 
Commissioning, Training and Handover Activities 
p)  What is the proposed approached to the staged Defects 
Liability Period activities? 
B 
Project Program (TRF 9) 
25%

that this program will demonstrate that the Tenderer has an 
understanding of the constraints on construction, lead times and 
procurement activities. 
The program is to comply with the milestones date layout within 
the RTF under 5.5.1. 
Details expected to be included are: 
a) 
How will your team a will manage the staging and 
handover timeframes and find efficiencies as the project 
progresses.   
b) 
How will your team manage long procurement items 
c) 
Site establishment and demolition are to start onsite 
whilst the due diligence is being completed.

4.3. 
Security, Probity and Financial Checks 
4.3.1. 
The Department may perform such security, probity and financial investigations and 
procedures as it may determine are necessary in relation to Tenderers, their 
employees, officers, partners, associates, subcontractors or related entities and their 
officers, employees and subcontractors. 
4.3.2. 
Tenderers will be expected to provide reasonable assistance to the Department 
regarding such checks, including supplying further information as requested. 

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 27  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
4.3.3. 
Any omission or failure to provide detailed information will diminish the scoring ability 
of the submission and may result in the Department removing the Tender from 
further consideration.  
4.3.4. 
Tenders will be assessed in accordance with the following evaluation process. 
4.4. 
Step 1 – Screening 
4.4.1. 
Tenderers will be assessed to ensure they comply with the mandatory Minimum 
Content and Format Requirements, the mandatory Conditions for Participation and 
the essential requirements.  
4.4.2. 
Subject to clause 3.13, any failure by a Tenderer to meet these requirements will 
result in that Tender being excluded from further evaluation. Relevant Tenderers will 
NOT be notified that its tender was not compliant and will not proceed further in the 
evaluation process. 
4.5. 
Step 2 – Technical Requirements 
4.5.1. 
Tenders that are not excluded from further evaluation at Step 1 will be assessed 
against the technical criteria as required by Tender Evaluation Criteria Response 
Form (TRF) 9 Response to Technical Requirements.  
4.5.2. 
Tenders should provide enough detail against each technical Criterion to ensure that 
the Department has a clear understanding of the business solution being offered and 
that the complete Requirements have been addressed.  
4.5.3. 
Unclear or contradictory statements will result in a low scoring assessment against 
those criteria.  
4.5.4. 
Presentations and/or site visits may be requested/conducted if required.  The 
Department has the discretion during the evaluation of Tenders to hold discussions 
and site visits with any or all Tenderers, including any proposed sub-contractors of a 
Tenderer, in order to seek further clarification of their Tender. 
4.5.5. 
If at the conclusion of the technical assessment, the Department determines a 
shortlist of Tenders, only those Tenders that have been shortlisted will proceed to 
the next step of the evaluation. 
4.6. 
Step 3 – Cost Evaluation  
4.6.1. 
Tenders that have been shortlisted following Step 2 will be assessed on the pricing 
details provided in the Tender Response Form (TRF) 11: Pricing Schedule.  
4.6.2. 
The Department reserves the right to seek clarification on any pricing matters and to 
seek a best and final offer in respect of pricing from each Tenderer that reaches 
Step 3, if it is deemed necessary, to determine a clear value for money outcome for 
the Department. 
4.7. 
Step 4 – Assessment of Unweighted Evaluation Criteria 
4.7.1. 
Economic Benefit to the Australian Economy Assessment (if applicable) 
a. 
The Department will undertake an assessment of each Tenderer’s response 
addressing Economic Benefit to the Australian Economy Tender Response 
Form (TRF) 10. 
 
 

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 28  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
4.7.2. 
Financial Viability 
a. 
The Tenderers that are shortlisted will be assessed on their financial Viability. 
The Department will take into consideration: 
i. 
The Department will undertake an assessment of each of the Tenderer’s 
responses addressing the Financial Viability Tender Response Form 
(TRF4) using the principals outlined by the Department of Finances 
guidance. Assessing Financial Viability | Department of Finance. And may 
seek a third party assessment be undertaken to provide an additional 
assessment. 
ii. 
If there are any disclosures that could affect/impact the successful delivery 
of the contract. 
4.7.3. 
Compliance with Draft Contract Criterion  
a. 
The Department will undertake an assessment of each Tenderer’s response 
addressing Compliance with Draft Contract Tender Response Form (TRF) 6. 
4.7.4. 
Modern Slavery 
a. 
The Department will undertake an assessment of each Tenderer’s response 
addressing Modern Slavery Tender Response Form (TRF) 12. 
4.7.5. 
Sustainable Procurement  
a. 
The Department will undertake an assessment of each Tenderer’s response 
addressing Sustainable Procurement Tender Response Form (TRF) 13. 
4.7.6. 
Australian Skills Guarantee Targets (if applicable) 
a. 
In evaluating Tenders, the Department will take into consideration: 
i. 
the Tenderer's past performance and reporting in accordance with the 
Australian Skills Guarantee Procurement Connected Policy (if any); 
ii. 
the extent to which the Tenderer demonstrates its proposed targets would 
increase the proportion of Apprentices and ICT Cadets engaged in the 
workforce of the Tenderer (including its subcontractors). 
Note to Tenderers: A Tenderer that has no past experience with any Australian 
Skills Guarantee Targets will not be disadvantaged by this alone. 
4.8. 
Step 5 – Value for Money  
4.8.1. 
The Department will determine best value for money by undertaking a comparative 
analysis of the relevant financial and non-financial costs and benefits of the Tenders, 
including an assessment of risk.   
4.8.2. 
In determining value for money, the Department will evaluate Tenders against the 
evaluation criteria and consider: 
a. 
the assessment scores through the technical assessment;   
b. 
the results of referee checks, site visits or presentations (if conducted);  
c. 
any risks to the Department posed by the Tender, including the degree of 
compliance with the draft Contract, financial viability of the Tenderer, any 
identified WHS risks, and any identified legislative compliance issues (such as 
compliance with the National Construction Code to the extent applicable);  

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 29  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
d. 
the overall whole of life costs;  
e. 
the benefit to the Australian economy;  
f. 
the outcomes of any other assessed criteria; and 
g. 
any other matter that the Department considers relevant. 
4.9. 
Steps 6 and 7 – Preferred Tenderer and contract negotiations 
4.9.1. 
Following Step 5, the Department will notify the Tenderer who is determined to 
represent the best value for money (the Preferred Tenderer). The Department will 
enter into contract negotiations with the Preferred Tenderer. The Department 
reserves the right to enter into parallel negotiations with multiple preferred 
Tenderers. 
4.9.2. 
If the Department forms the view that negotiations are unlikely to achieve agreement 
on terms acceptable to the Department, the Department may approach the next 
ranked Tenderer to commence negotiations, such Tenderer will then become the 
Preferred Tenderer. 
4.10. 
Referee Checks 
4.10.1. 
Tenderers must ensure that nominated referees: 
a. 
do not have an actual or potential conflict of interest. In particular, referees must 
not: 
i. 
be an employee or holder of a current executive office within the Tenderer 
organisation or have a business association with the Tenderer or one of its 
subsidiaries; 
ii. 
be included in the Tender as proposed personnel; or 
iii. be a Former DFAT Employee; and 
b. 
in the case of current DFAT personnel, including current DFAT employees, are 
not involved with the RFT process; and 
c. 
are available to be contacted within 3 weeks of Closing Time.  
4.10.2. 
The Department may, at its discretion, contact any referee nominated by a Tenderer 
in its Tender, and may seek written or verbal comments from that referee. 
4.10.3. 
The Department may also seek information about any Tenderer from any other 
source, including from within the Commonwealth, whether or not the individuals or 
organisations contacted are nominated by the Tenderer. 
4.11. 
Tender Clarification 
4.11.1. 
At any stage the Department may ask a Tenderer to clarify or provide additional 
information on any aspect of its Tender. 
4.11.2. 
Failure to respond to a request for clarification in the manner requested by the 
Department may have an adverse impact on the evaluation of the Tender. 
4.12. 
Publication of Contract Award 
4.12.1. 
In accordance with the Senate Order on Entity Contracts, the Department is required 
to list the contracts with a consideration of $100,000 or more that it entered into 
during the previous 12 months, or that have not been fully performed, on the Internet 

 
PMSB 25007-C71 HWC RFT                                                                                                                                                  Page | 30  
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
with access via the Department’s home page.  Further information regarding these 
requirements is specified in Resource Management Guide No. 403 - Meeting the 
Senate Order for Entity Contracts. 
4.12.2. 
The Department is also required to publish details of contracts it enters into with an 
estimated liability of $10,000 or more on AusTender within 42 days of entering into 
the contract.  Further information about this requirement can be found at 
https://www.finance.gov.au/publications/resource-management-guides/procurement-
publishing-reporting-obligations-rmg-423.  
4.13. 
Debriefing 
4.13.1. 
Following the rejection of a tender, or the award of a Contract, Tenderers will be 
informed in writing of the outcome of their tender.  
4.13.2. 
Tenderers may, in writing, request a debrief concerning their tender, following the 
award of a Contract. 
4.13.3. 
Tenderers will be debriefed against the evaluation criteria set out in this RFT. A 
Tenderer will not be provided with information concerning other Tenders. No 
comparisons with other Tenders will be made.

PROBITY BRIEFING – Probity Arrangements
•
Australian Government Solicitor (AGS) has been appointed as DFAT’s Probity 
Adviser for DFAT’s RFT process for the provision of a Head Works Contractor 
for the refurbishment of the Australian High Commission Staff Residential 
Compound (SRC) Konedobu, Port Moresby, Papua New Guinea.
•
We are working with DFAT to ensure this procurement is conducted in a way 
that complies with probity principles and to provide independent oversight of 
the process.
•
Please note this industry briefing is to provide you with background 
information only.
•
Respondents should not rely on a statement made by DFAT in this industry 
briefing as amending the RFT, unless that is confirmed by DFAT in writing via 
an addendum.

PROBITY BRIEFING – Probity Arrangements
•
The industry briefing and any information provided to you should be treated 
as confidential information. 
•
Do not use any information provided to you for any purpose other than 
planning for, and participating, in this RFT process.
•
Respondents should not discuss the RFT with, or seek information regarding 
the RFT, from any of their business-as-usual DFAT contacts.
•
The industry briefing will be recorded. Copies of the industry briefing 
recording, presentations and responses to questions provided at the industry 
briefing will be provided to Tenderers through AusTender following the 
industry briefing.
•
Respondents may take written notes, but should not record the industry 
briefing (e.g. by screen snapshots, AI transcript, video recording etc).

PROBITY BRIEFING – Probity Arrangements
•
Please hold your questions until the Q&A session at the end of the industry briefing. 
•
Please direct your questions to the presenter in a clear way so everyone can hear it. 
•
Any questions and responses to these during the industry briefing will be made 
available to all Respondents via an addendum by email.
•
If any question cannot be accurately answered today, the question will be taken on 
notice and, where possible, answers provided via an addendum by email.

PROBITY BRIEFING – Probity Arrangements
•
Tenderers must notify the Department as soon as practicable after becoming 
aware of an actual or potential conflict of interest, or at any time prior to 
receiving notification of the award of a contract or the termination of the RFT 
process.
•
All inquiries or questions or other communications arising out of the RFT, or 
the procurement process more generally, should be directed to, and only to 
the RFT Contact – aopoprojects@dfat.gov.au

Legal Briefing 
REFURBISHMENT OF THE AUSTRALIAN HIGH COMMISSION STAFF 
RESIDENTIAL COMPOUND KONEDOBU, 
PORT MORESBY, PAPUA NEW GUINEA
PRESENTED BY
MARKO MISKO (PARTNER)
THOMAS APPLEBY (ASSOCIATE) 
HWLE LAWYERS 

Overview
⎖Introductory Remarks
⎖Key Contract Feature – Due Diligence Phase
⎖Tender Submission – Due Diligence Phase
⎖General Comments on Tender Process and Conforming Tender Requirements
⎖Closing Remarks and Questions

Introductory Remarks
⎖Overview of key legal issues only
⎖The materials and all matters discussed are for information purposes only and have no legal precedence
⎖This legal brief is no substitute for reading the entirety of the tender and contract documents
⎖Do not rely on your understanding of other tender / contract documents
⎖DFAT tendering requirements are very particular – it is critical to understand and comply with them

Key Contract Feature – Stage based Due Diligence Phase
⎖Amended AS4902-2000 Design and Construct
⎖A new ‘Due Diligence Phase’ (Clause 6) having regard to the Scope of Requirements (Part 2 of the RFT)
⎖Purpose of the Due Diligence Phase: 
⎻Due to the age and disparate condition of the buildings at Konedobu, and the limited access to undertake extensive investigations of individual 
residences while still occupied, the scope or works includes a component of due diligence to ensure that individual apartment differences are 
captured typically around existing services.
⎻DFAT will provide Design drawings to 90%. - The contractor is responsible to undertake due diligence review to uplift the drawings to 100%/ IFC 
drawings and opportunities of innovation for the Contractor to incorporate.
⎖The Contract and Works is divided into Stages 2–8. Each Stage has its own:
⎻Date for Delivery Phase Approval; and
⎻Date for Practical Completion Date
⎖The Due Diligence process will be conducted on a stage-by-stage (sliding) basis, rather than across the entire project at once.
⎖For each stage, Due Diligence must commence promptly, and no later than 2 business days after access to the relevant Site is provided.

Due Diligence Phase
⎖The Due Diligence Phase for a Stage is the period:
⎻Commencing on the Date of Contract; and
⎻Ending on the earlier of:
•
the Date of Delivery Phase Approval for the Stage; or
•
the date of a notice under the Contract ending the process.
⎖During this phase, the Contractor undertakes Due Diligence of:
⎻The Preliminary Design; and
⎻The Site.

Contractor’s Core Obligation
⎖The Contractor must, for each Stage, undertake a proactive and comprehensive due diligence of each Preliminary Design and 
Site by physically inspecting each Site to:
⎻Identify and propose all Buildability Input;
⎻Identify and propose all Innovation Input;
⎻Identify and propose all Risk Reduction Input; and
⎻Review, discuss and agree that input with the Superintendent.
Required Outcomes
⎖The Contractor is required to:
⎻Identify Buildability Issues;
⎻Identify Design Errors;
⎻Review cost and program requirements;
⎻Identify material risks; and
⎻Prepare the Due Diligence Report.

How the Due Diligence Process Operates
Clause 6 Process - Due Diligence of Preliminary Design and Site for each Stage
Identify:
⎖Buildability Issues; Innovation opportunities; Project risks
Prepare:
⎖Buildability Input; Innovation Input; Risk Reduction Input
Update:
⎖Cost Plans; Programs
Prepare:
⎖Due Diligence Report
Negotiate adjustments with the Department
Delivery Phase Approval decision

Due Diligence Input
Due Diligence Input consists of:
⎖Buildability Input
⎻Input provided to address Buildability Issues.
⎖Innovation Input
⎻Input which a prudent, competent and experienced contractor would provide to maximise Project Objectives.
⎖Risk Reduction Input
⎻Input which a prudent, competent and experienced contractor would provide to identify and address material risks that may 
arise during the Delivery Phase.

Buildability Review
Buildability Issues
⎖A Buildability Issue includes:
⎻Any aspect of the Preliminary Design causing a problem, difficulty or complexity relating to:
•
the means, methods or techniques by which the WUC are performed;
•
the coordination or integration of the WUC at the Site; or
•
the connection or existence of services to or at the Site; and
⎻Any Design Error.
⎖Contractor Must
⎻Identify all Buildability Issues
⎻Provide Buildability Input.
⎻Advise how Buildability Issues should be resolved.
⎻Ensure the Due Diligence Report incorporates all required Buildability Input.

Design Verification
Design Verification
⎖Design Verification means the Due Diligence of the Design Documents undertaken by the Contractor.
Design Error
⎖A Design Error includes: errors; omissions; ambiguity; inadequacy; inconsistency; incompleteness; or lack of coordination or 
integration, within the Preliminary Design.
⎖The Contractor's Due Diligence includes identifying and proposing responses to Design Errors.

Innovation Input
⎖The Contractor must provide all input which a prudent, competent and experienced contractor would reasonably provide to 
maximise the Project Objectives.
⎖Innovation Input includes:
⎻Cost planning recommendations; and
⎻Programming recommendations.
⎖The Contractor must review, discuss and agree Innovation Input with the Superintendent.
⎖Project Objectives means the objectives set out in or reasonably to be inferred from the Principal's Project Requirements 
(Annexure Part F of the Contract);

Risk Reduction Input
⎖The Contractor must provide all input which a prudent, competent and experienced contractor would reasonably provide to 
ensure material risks are:
⎻Identified; and
⎻Addressed during the Due Diligence Phase.
⎖Examples include:
⎻Asbestos; and
⎻Other hazardous materials at the Site.
⎖The Contractor may recommend risk reduction studies and may be required to undertake those studies if directed.

Cost Planning Obligations
⎖The Contractor must:
⎻Review the Cost Plan while undertaking Due Diligence.
⎻Ensure construction cost accords with the Cost Plan.
⎻Ensure construction cost does not exceed the Delivery Phase Price for each Stage, except where permitted under the 
Contract.
⎻Provide Innovation Input relating to cost outcomes.
⎻Provide Risk Reduction Input relating to cost risks.
⎻Update Cost Plans.
⎻Submit updated Cost Plans to the Superintendent.
⎻Revise Cost Plans as directed.
⎻Obtain Superintendent approval.

Programming Obligations
⎖The Contractor must:
⎻Review the Program while undertaking Due Diligence.
⎻Ensure construction duration does not exceed the Date for Practical Completion for each Stage except where permitted 
under the Contract.
⎻Ensure compliance with the Master Program.
⎻Provide Innovation Input relating to programme outcomes.
⎻Provide Risk Reduction Input relating to programme risks.
⎻Update Programs.
⎻Submit updated Programs.
⎻Revise Programs as directed.
⎻Obtain Superintendent approval.

Due Diligence Report
⎖The Contractor must prepare a Due Diligence Report.
⎖The report must:
⎻Be in a form reasonably required by the Superintendent;
⎻Contain all Due Diligence Input; and
⎻Be approved by the Superintendent.
⎖Purpose of the Report
⎖To capture:
⎻Buildability Input;
⎻Innovation Input; and
⎻Risk Reduction Input,
     to be implemented in finalising the Preliminary Design at the commencement of the Delivery Phase.
⎖Submission Process: Draft Due Diligence Report; Revised drafts (if required) and Final draft Due Diligence Report.

Delivery Phase Approval for each Stage
Prior to Delivery Phase Approval for a Stage
⎖The Contractor must:
⎻Notify proposed adjustments (if any) to:
•
commencement dates; 
•
the Preliminary Design; 
•
Provisional Allowances; 
•
Delivery Phase Price; and 
•
Date for Practical Completion.
⎻Negotiate those matters with the Department.
⎻Provide costing and supporting information required by the Superintendent.

Delivery Phase Approval for each Stage
⎖If Delivery Phase Approval is achieved for a Stage
⎻Department issues a Delivery Phase Approval notice.
⎻Contract amendments are executed.
⎻Contractor proceeds with the remainder of the WUC including design, construction, commissioning, completion and 
handover.
⎖If Delivery Phase Approval is not achieved for a Stage
⎻Contractor has no entitlement to undertake the Delivery Phase.
⎻Contractor is entitled only to the relevant Due Diligence Phase Price payable under the Contract.
⎻Contractor must hand over Contract Documents and assist with transition.
⎻Department may re-tender or otherwise procure the Works.

What the RFT is seeking from Tenderer
⎖Evaluation Criteria – Approach to Due Diligence. 
⎖Tenderers must demonstrate:
⎻A proactive approach to preparation, submission, review and development of design documentation.
⎻Capability to undertake all design-related tasks and activities.
⎻Ability to undertake design Due Diligence concurrently with demolition activities.
⎻Ability to manage Due Diligence across a rolling staged program of works.

RFT Pricing Requirements for Due Diligence
⎖Pricing Structure 
⎖Tenderers must provide, for each Stage:
⎻Due Diligence Phase Price.
⎻Delivery Phase Price.
⎻Provisional Allowance (Cost).
⎖Due Diligence Pricing Breakdown
⎖Provisional Allowance
⎻Available for unexpected and unforeseeable changes arising from the Due Diligence Phase, subject to validation and 
approval by the Superintendent.

General Comments on Tender Process
⎖Access to Due Diligence Information and Design (TC 3.3) (Attachment C of the RFT)
⎖Lodgement and Closing Time / Late Tenders 
⎻Closing Time is 13 August 2026 at 1700hrs Canberra Local Time  (TC 3.2 and 3.6)
⎖Questions (TC 3.43) 
⎻The Department will not respond to any request for clarification in relation to the RFT from a Tenderer received after the Deadline for Questions - 
6 August 2026 at 1700hrs Canberra Local Time. (TC 3.1.10)
⎖Validity Period – 180 Days (TC 3.2.5)
⎖Essential Requirements (TC 3.10) 
⎖Debrief for unsuccessful applicants – following the award of a Contract (TC 4.12)
⎖Unintentional Errors of Form (TC 3.13)
⎖Joint Tenders (TC 3.16)
⎖Alternative Tenderers (TC 3.18)

Conditions for Participation and Minimum Content and Format 
Requirements
⎖Conditions for Participation (TC 3.11) 
⎻Tenderers must exist as a single legal entity at the Closing Time. 
⎻The Tenderer must hold all Valid and satisfactory Statements of Tax record (STRs) required for its entity type. 
⎻The Tenderer must hold all STRs for the entity type of any first-tier subcontractor that it proposes, as part of its Tender, to engage to deliver 
goods or services with an estimated value of over $4 million (GST inclusive). 
⎖Minimum Content and Format Requirements (TC 3.12) 
⎻Tenders must be written in English and all measurements must be expressed in Australian legal units of measurement;
⎻Tenders must contain completed and detailed responses to all TRFs 1 to 16 inclusive of all attachments, substantially in the form provided in TRFs 
1 to 16
⎻Tenders must contain a completed and executed Tenderer’s deed poll substantially in the form provided with this RFT at TRF 8; 
⎻Tenders must contain a completed Pricing Schedule substantially in the form provided with this RFT, at TRF 11;
⎻Tenders must include all STRs relevant to the Tenderer’s entity type; and
⎻Tenders must include all STRs for any first-tier subcontractor that it proposes to engage to deliver goods or services. 

Closing remarks and 
questions
NEVER DIE WONDERING… SEEK 
CLARIFICATION WHERE REQUIRED

MANDATORY CONDITION – STATEMENT OF TAX 
RECORD
• Tenderers should apply for a Statement of Tax Record and should ensure that 
their subcontractors apply for a Statement of Tax Record within sufficient time 
to meet these mandatory Conditions for Participation.
• Does your business have an ABN or TFN
• If the Tenderer or their subcontractor is:
• a trust, a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record must be provided in respect 
of the trust and trustee;
• a partnership, a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record must be requested 
on behalf of the partnership and in respect of each partner that will be 
directly involved in the delivery of the contract;
• a joint venture, a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record must be provided in 
respect of each party to the joint venture; and
• a subsidiary of a tax consolidated group, or a Multiple Entry Consolidated 
group, a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record must be provided by the 
subsidiary and the head company.

MANDATORY CONDITION – STATEMENT OF TAX 
RECORD
A tenderer that is a new Australian business or a foreign 
company, and who does not have a TFN or ABN, must still 
apply for and submit a Statement of Tax Record for the 
purpose of complying to these Mandatory Conditions of 
Participation.

MANDATORY CONDITIONS

PROJECT OVERVIEW

PROJECT OVERVIEW

PROJECT OVERVIEW

Left: looking up over the Kone 
Compound
Below: Looking from Tower D 
down over Stage 2 & the 
Kondobu Compound

House- 3 & 4 bedrooms
Dwelling Typologies
Apartments- 3 & 4 bedrooms
Duplex-  4 bedrooms

•
The object of the Project is to upgrade and modernise all the 39 properties 
and to bring them up to a fit-for-purpose condition and to current Australian 
standards. 
•
The intent of the refurbishment is to use durable, climate-appropriate 
materials that can be maintained locally or within the region. 
•
The design standardizes layouts, finishes fixtures and plant, to improve 
maintainability. The design applies a repeatable “kit-of-parts” approach to 
enable interchangeable components and efficient repairs where-ever 
possible. 
•
Compliance with DFAT technical briefs and specifications
DESIGN OVERVIEW

SCOPE OF WORKS
Demolition
•
Demolition of internal surfaces, including flooring, walls, ceilings, joinery and 
redundant and old services.
•
Demolition and removal of external services including AC units and 
covers/conduits, Solar Hot water systems etc
•
Removal and treatment of mouldy plasterboard walls and ceilings.
New
•
New wall linings, and insulation to ceilings and selected walls
•
Supply and install new kitchens and appliances, relocate services to suit new 
layout and appliances.
•
Supply and install new robes, walk in robes, while working around existing security 
hardware
•
Refurbishment of wet areas (bathrooms, powder rooms, and laundries), including 
preparation of floors and walls, waterproofing, relocate services as required, 
supply and fit new tiles and fitments.

•
Preparation of floor for new tiles throughout, noting that each residence has 
inconsistent floor levels (potentially to each room). Where inconsistent floor levels are 
present additional floor treatment/ leveling will be required to standardise design and 
layouts and eliminate trip hazards.
•
Replace and relocate extraction/exhaust fans in bathrooms, laundry and kitchen, 
moving all fans from glazing units to either the walls or ceilings.
•
Relocate and replace existing air-conditioning units with new energy-efficient 
condensate air conditioning systems/reverse cycle air conditioning systems and
•
Replace/add light fittings and switch plates, electrical outlets, all existing power and 
switch cover plates for a consistent installation.
•
Electrical re-wiring if there are any non-compliance discovered with the existing 
installation. Inclusion of surge protection to be incorporate into switch boards.
•
replace faceplates and ensure operability of all TV aerial outlets.
•
Upgrade phone and data cable connection points, and install a conduit for future 
capability upgrade from roof to living room.
•
Replace solar hot water system
•
Replace/ upgrade fire services Including replacement of fire hydrants and blankets.

•
New stair handrails and balustrades, and refurbish the existing stair treads
•
Safety barrier to the upper level safe haven gate (stairs).
•
New paint throughout.
•
Rectification of any damage to the external painted brick façade as a result of the works.
•
New double glazing and security screens to window openings, 
•
Curtains, blinds and window furniture.



PROGRAMME OVERVIEW
• The works will be carried as a staged rolling program.
• Due to the requirement to continue to house residents on the site, it is 
proposed that between 4-6 dwellings are refurbished at any one time to 
enable a rolling staged program of works across 7 remaining stages.
• Australian High commission Staff and their families will continue to reside 
onsite for the duration of the Project. For that reason security and safety and 
safe work practices will be priorities for DFAT. 
• Quality to minimise defects to ensure minimal delays to residents during 
Defect liability period
• The staging plan is indicative and is subject to change to respond to 
operational requirements. As the project progresses the staging plan may be 
reviewed and amended to meet operational needs.

PROPOSED STAGING

ATTACHMENT E: DUE DILIGENCE DEED POLL OF CONFIDENTIALITY 
Availability of Due Diligence Information
For reasons of security and confidentiality, and subject to paragraph 3.3 of Part 1 – Conditions of Tender, the Due Diligence Information will not be made available to Tenderers until the requirements in clause 4.1(a) of the Due Diligence Information Deed Poll of Confidentiality are satisfied.  
The Deed Poll of Confidentiality - Due Diligence Information (Confidentiality Deed Poll) is available on AusTender as part of this RFT (ATTACHMENT E – DUE DILIGENCE DEED POLL OF CONFIDENTIALITY) and must be completed and returned to the Contact Email stated in the RFT. Following, which the Department will arrange for provision of the relevant Due Diligence Information, subject to paragraph 3.3 of Part 1 – Conditions of Tender. Tenderers that do not submit a correctly completed and properly executed Confidentiality Deed Poll in accordance with the submission requirements of this Attachment E will not be provided access to the Due Diligence Information including that contained in Attachment C – Due Diligence Information (Design Documentation). Any Tender submitted without reference to the information in Attachment C may be excluded from participation in this RFT process
If requested by DFAT, persons seeking to access Due Diligence Information must provide DFAT with reasonable evidence (as determined by DFAT) to substantiate their identity as an employee, contractor or agent of the relevant Tenderer(s) (such as an official email address or a delegation).  
DFAT may require that any documents (including Due Diligence Information) be securely destroyed in accordance with paragraph 3.3.9 of Part 1 – Conditions of Tender of the RFT. 
Provision of Due Diligence Information will be through a secure AusTender page with restricted access. Tenderers should note that any further DFAT Confidential Information will also be published on the same secure AusTender page with restricted access.
Confidentiality Deed Poll to be submitted
Subject to Part 1 – Conditions of Tender including this Attachment E, DFAT will provide:
 the information contained in Attachment C – Due Diligence Information (Design Documentation)) of the Request for Tender (RFT), and
any additional DFAT Confidential Information, 
to Tenderers who submit the Confidentiality Deed Poll.. 
Eligibility to receive Due Diligence Information
To be eligible to receive DFAT Confidential Information, including the Due Diligence Information, Tenderers must satisfy the requirements in clause 4.1(a) of the Due Diligence Information Confidentiality Deed Poll, which are:
the Tenderer correctly completing, properly executing and submitting the Confidentiality Deed Poll;
the Tenderer being assessed by DFAT as having relevant prior experience in the provision of similar services; and 
the Tenderer being assessed by DFAT as having a satisfactory security risk profile.
DFAT may require additional information to substantiate the Tenderer’s compliance with clause 3(b) and (c) above. 
If DFAT determines in its sole and absolute discretion that the Confidentiality Deed Poll submitted by an entity does not satisfy all the requirements of this clause 3, DFAT may refuse to provide access to DFAT Confidential Information, including the Due Diligence Information to that individual or entity. 
If a Tenderer is denied access to the DFAT Confidential Information including the Due Diligence Information and they wish to dispute DFAT’s decision, the Tenderer may lodge a complaint in accordance with Part A – Conditions of Tender.
How to submit the Confidentiality Deed Poll
The Confidentiality Deed Poll must be scanned and sent via email to the Contact Email:  aopoprojects@dfat.gov.au
Tenderers that do not submit the Confidentiality Deed Poll in accordance with the submission requirements above will not be provided DFAT Confidential Information, including the Due Diligence Information contained in Attachment C – Due Diligence Information (Design Documentation).
DFAT will notify a Tenderer if DFAT considers that its Confidentiality Deed Poll does not comply with requirements in order to give the Tenderer an opportunity to correct any submission errors or provide evidence for DFAT’s consideration.
