Due Diligence Information Deed Poll of Confidentiality 
It is agreed: 
Definitions and interpretation clauses
Definitions
In this deed poll:
Interpretation
In this deed poll unless a contrary intention is expressed:
other parts of speech and grammatical forms of a word or phrase defined in this deed have a corresponding meaning;
a reference to a 'person' includes any individual, firm, company, partnership, joint venture, an unincorporated body or association, trust, corporation or other body corporate and any Government Agency (whether or not having a separate legal personality);
a reference to any thing (including any right) includes a part of that thing, but nothing in this clause 1.2(c) implies that performance of part of an obligation constitutes performance of the obligation;
a reference to a clause, party, annexure, exhibit or schedule is a reference to a clause of, and a party, annexure, exhibit and schedule to, this deed poll and a reference to this deed poll includes any clause, annexure, exhibit and schedule;
a reference to a document (including this deed poll) includes all amendments or supplements to, or replacements or novations of, that document;
a reference to a party to any document includes that party's successors and permitted assigns;
a reference to time is to Canberra, ACT, Australia time;
a reference to any legislation includes all delegated legislation made under it and includes all amendments, consolidations, replacements or re-enactments of any of them, from time to time;
a reference to an agreement other than this deed poll includes an undertaking, deed, agreement or legally enforceable arrangement or understanding whether or not in writing;
a reference to a document includes any agreement or contract in writing, or any certificate, notice, deed, instrument or other document of any kind;
a provision of this deed poll may not be construed adversely to a party solely on the ground that the party (or that party's representative) was responsible for the preparation of this deed poll or the preparation or proposal of that provision;
a reference to a body, other than a party to this deed poll (including an institute, association or authority), whether statutory or not, which ceases to exist or whose powers or functions are transferred to another body, is a reference to the body which replaces it or which substantially succeeds to its powers or functions;
the words 'include', 'including', 'for example', 'such as' or any form of those words or similar expressions in this deed poll do not limit what else is included and must be construed as if they are followed by the words 'without limitation', unless there is express wording to the contrary;
the contra proferentem rule does not apply; 
a reference to a day is to the period of time commencing at  and ending 24 hours later; 
if a period of time is specified and dates from a day or the day of an act, event or circumstance, that period is to be determined exclusive of that day;
if an act or event must occur or be performed on or by a specified day and occurs or is performed after 5.00 pm on that day, it is taken to have occurred or been done on the next day; and
a reference to '$', 'A$', 'AUD', 'dollars' or 'Dollars' is a reference to the lawful currency of the Commonwealth of Australia.
Benefit
The Tenderer executed this deed poll for the benefit of DFAT. 
Confidentiality 	
Treatment of Confidential Information 
The Tenderer must: 
keep confidential any and all Confidential Information; 
not disclose the Confidential Information to anyone other than: 
its Personnel in accordance with clause 3.2; 
with the prior written consent of DFAT; or 
where required by applicable Laws, subject to the Tenderer giving DFAT reasonable notice of any such legal requirements or order to enable DFAT to seek a protective order or other appropriate remedy (unless this would be in violation of a court order or other legal requirement); 
only use the Confidential Information for the Permitted Purpose; 
not reproduce in any form any part of the Confidential Information other than for the Permitted Purpose without DFAT's prior written consent; and 
comply with any direction or request of DFAT in respect of the Confidential Information (including a request to prevent or cease any breach of this deed poll). 
Disclosure 
The Tenderer may disclose the Confidential Information in connection with the Permitted Purpose to its Personnel who: 
have a specific need to have access to the Confidential Information for the Permitted Purpose; 
are aware of the obligations of this Deed Poll; and
are under an enforceable obligation of confidentiality to the Tenderer. 
The Tenderer is liable for: 
any breaches of this deed poll by its Personnel; and 
any breaches of the individual deed poll of confidentiality by its Personnel. 
Security 
Notwithstanding any other provision under this deed, the Tenderer must (and must ensure its Personnel will) take all reasonable steps to avoid unauthorised access to the Due Diligence Information and unauthorised access to any of the Confidential Information.
Records 
The Tenderer must: 
maintain a record of its Personnel who have received Confidential Information; and 
upon DFAT's request and within 3 Business Days of the date of the DFAT's request, provide DFAT with a copy of the record maintained under clause 3.4(a) of this deed poll. 
Breach of obligations 
If the Tenderer becomes aware of any actual, threatened or suspected breach of this deed poll, including by any of its Personnel, the Tenderer must:
immediately notify DFAT in writing and take all steps necessary to remedy, prevent or stop the actual, threatened or suspected breach of this deed poll and comply with any reasonable directions issued by DFAT regarding any unauthorised use or disclosure of the Confidential Information or Personal Information; and
provide such other assistance as may be reasonably required by DFAT, including in relation to any claim or proceedings that DFAT may bring against any third party for unauthorised use or disclosure of the Confidential Information or Personal Information.
Injunctive relief 
The Tenderer acknowledges and agrees that: 
damages may not be an adequate remedy for a breach of this deed poll; and 
that DFAT is entitled to seek injunctive relief as a remedy for any breach or threatened breach of this deed poll by the Tenderer, in addition to any other remedies available at Law or in equity under, or independently of, this deed poll. 
Due Diligence Information 
Accessing the Due Diligence Information
Access to the Due Diligence Information will only be granted upon:
the Tenderer correctly completing and properly executing and submitting this deed poll; 
the Tenderer being assessed by DFAT as having relevant prior experience in the provision of similar services; and 
the Tenderer being assessed by DFAT as having a satisfactory security risk profile.
An unacceptable level of risk is when the identified security risks cannot be mitigated to a reasonable or  acceptable level, or the security risks to the Australian Government or its people, information or resources are too great. This includes where the security risks cannot be quantified or are too complex to be calculated. Risks considered by DFAT will include those outlined in the Australian Government Protective Security Policy Framework Part 6 (Third Party Risk Management) and Part 7 (Countering Foreign Interference and Espionage). 
In assessing requirements under clauses 4.1(a)(ii) and 4.1(a)(iii) DFAT may have regard to:
publicly available searches, information and records; 
DFAT internal security advice; and
security advice from other Commonwealth agencies. 
The Tenderer acknowledges and agrees that: 
DFAT is not liable for, and DFAT excludes any acts or omissions of the Due Diligence Information Provider; 
 the Tenderer's access to the Due Diligence Information may be subject to additional terms; and 
access to and availability of the Due Diligence Information is not guaranteed and it may not be able to be accessed or available at any time.
Due Diligence Information 
All information provided to the Tenderer by DFAT in the Due Diligence Information (notwithstanding whether or not given orally or in writing), including:
links and passwords to access the Due Diligence Information;
information in the Due Diligence Information (including any missing or incomplete documents subsequently provided to the Tenderer any additional material put into the Due Diligence Information); and
any information provided through the Due Diligence Information in response to a query from the Tenderer in the Due Diligence Information,
constitutes Confidential Information for the purposes of this deed poll. 
Prohibited use
The Tenderer and its Personnel are not permitted to:
view, access or retain any documents (in hard copy or electronic format) after the Tenderer or its Personnel's access to the Due Diligence Information has been terminated or expired, or during any period of suspension;
alter, mark, modify, damage or destroy any of the documents in the repository of the Due Diligence Information; and
permit any other person to do any of the acts specified in clauses 4.3(a), or 4.3(b), 
without DFAT's prior written consent.
Contents of the Due Diligence Information
The Tenderer acknowledges that DFAT has, at is discretion, uploaded or provided Confidential Information in the Due Diligence Information in relation to the RFT.
In making the Due Diligence Information available to the Tenderer DFAT does not warrant the accuracy, currency or completeness of the documents or contents of the Due Diligence Information.
The Due Diligence Information includes and/or will include materials relevant or in connection with the RFT, but is not to be taken as, or relied upon as, a complete or comprehensive representation of the information relevant to the RFT. 
Non Reliance
The Tenderer acknowledges that DFAT makes no representation or warranty in relation to the documents contained in, or contents of, the Due Diligence Information and that the Tenderer has in no way relied on any of the information or documents contained in the Due Diligence Information.  
Release
The Tenderer releases DFAT from any Claim of arising from or in connection with its use of the Due Diligence Information.
Return of Confidential Information 
Return or destruction of Confidential Information
Subject to clause 5.1(b), if requested by DFAT, the Tenderer must:
promptly return or promptly destroy all documents and other physical records of Confidential Information in the Tenderer's possession, custody, power or control (including its Personnel);
delete the Confidential Information from any computer system or other device operated, controlled or which may be accessed by the Tenderer; and
provide written confirmation (or if required by DFAT, a statutory declaration) to DFAT confirming that all those records and any copies of Confidential Information have been returned, destroyed or erased, as appropriate.
The obligations in clause 5.1(a) to return, destroy or erase Confidential Information do not extend to:
legal advice, legal opinions or reports prepared by the Tenderer's advisers in connection with the RFT; or
documents or other materials that are required to be retained in order to comply with any relevant Laws, professional obligations, insurance requirements, audit requirements or order of any government agency for so long as it is necessary to satisfy the relevant requirements.
If the Tenderer retains any Confidential Information pursuant to clause 5.1(b), the Tenderer must maintain the confidentiality of such Confidential Information in accordance with the terms of this deed.
The return, destruction or deletion of Confidential Information in accordance with this clause 5.1 does not release the Tenderer, or its Personnel from the obligations imposed on the Tenderer under this deed poll. 
Privacy and Personal Information 
Privacy Act 
If the Confidential Information contains any Personal Information, the Tenderer must: 
comply with the Privacy Act 1988 (Cth) and any other applicable privacy laws or data protection laws, regulations or codes; 
promptly notify DFAT of any complaints or investigations under or in connection with any of the laws, regulations or codes referred to in clause 6.1(a). 
Audit 
Audit
The Tenderer acknowledges and accepts that any person accessing the Due Diligence Information leaves an auditable trail, including:
the amount of times an individual has accessed, viewed or downloaded a document in the Due Diligence Information; and
the date and time of access.
The Commonwealth may conduct audits in relation to access to the Due Diligence Information from time to time, and the Tenderer agrees to provide DFAT with such access as required to enable DFAT to conduct audits (including providing DFAT with access to the relevant members of its Personnel). 
The Tenderer must keep a written record of all of its Personnel who has accessed or has been granted access to the Due Diligence Information and shall promptly provide the record to DFAT upon request.
Indemnity and liability 
Indemnity 
The Tenderer indemnifies DFAT against all Claims suffered or incurred (howsoever arising) by DFAT as a result of any breach of this deed poll by the Tenderer, or any of its Personnel.
Termination or suspension 
Without limitation to any other right or remedy it may have, DFAT reserves the right to terminate or suspend access to the Due Diligence Information, in whole or in part, by the Tenderer, or any of its Personnel, at any time without notice and without giving any reason.
General
Governing law and jurisdiction
This deed poll is governed by and will be construed according to the laws of the Australian Capital Territory.
The parties irrevocably submit to and accept the non-exclusive jurisdiction of the courts and appellate courts of the Australian Capital Territory.
No exclusion of law or equity
This deed poll must not be construed to exclude the operation of any principle of law or equity, including in relation to the protection and preservation of the confidentiality of Confidential Information. 
Further acts
Each party will promptly do and perform all further acts and execute and deliver all further documents (in form and content reasonably satisfactory to that party) required by Law or reasonably requested by any other party to give effect to this deed poll.
Expenses
Each party will pay its own costs and expenses in connection with the negotiation, preparation, execution and performance of this deed poll and access to and review of the information in the Due Diligence Information.
No representation or reliance
Each party acknowledges that no party (nor any person acting on its behalf) has made any representation or other inducement to it to enter into this deed poll, except for representations or inducements expressly set out in this deed poll.
Each party acknowledges and confirms that it does not enter into this deed poll in reliance on any representation or other inducement by or on behalf of any other party, except for any representation or inducement expressly set out in this deed poll and has entered into this deed on reliance of its own enquiries and due diligence.
Amendment
This deed poll may be amended only by a document signed by all parties.
No waiver
No waiver by DFAT of one breach of any obligation or provision herein contained or implied will operate as a waiver of another breach of the same or of any other obligation or provision herein contained or implied.
None of the provisions in this deed poll will be taken either at law or in equity to have been varied, waived, discharged or released by DFAT unless by its express consent in writing. 
Continuing obligation
The obligations of the Tenderer under this deed poll continue until the Confidential Information loses its quality of confidentiality.

TENDER RESPONSE FORMS
Tender Submission 
Tenderers must ensure that their Tenders include as a minimum, the completion of each of the Tender Response Forms (TRF) that form this Annexure. 
The tender response forms have text highlighted in yellow- this has been done to help tenderers identify where they need to provide details. Prior to submitting please ensure that the text is no longer highlighted and any guidance notes in blue text is deleted.
Tender Response Form (TRF) 1: Mandatory Conditions for Participation and Minimum Content and Format Requirements
Mandatory Conditions for Participation
Outline of Mandatory Conditions for Participation
The Mandatory Conditions for Participation are:
Note to Tenderers: 
Tenderers should apply for a Statement of Tax Record and should ensure that their subcontractors apply for a Statement of Tax Record within sufficient time to meet these mandatory Conditions for Participation. If the Tenderer or their subcontractor is:
a trust, a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record must be provided in respect of the trust and trustee;
a partnership, a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record must be requested on behalf of the partnership and in respect of each partner that will be directly involved in the delivery of the contract;
a joint venture, a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record must be provided in respect of each party to the joint venture; and
a subsidiary of a tax consolidated group, or a Multiple Entry Consolidated group, a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record must be provided by the subsidiary and the head company.
Depending on their entity type, some Tenderers may need to apply for multiple Statements of Tax Record. 
A tenderer that is a new Australian business or a foreign company, and who does not have a TFN or ABN, must still apply for and submit a Statement of Tax Record for the purpose of complying to these Mandatory Conditions of Participation. 
Tenderers that are a new Australian business or a foreign company can find more information on applying for a Statement of Tax Record on the Australian Taxation Office website, under ‘ Requesting and STR - Not registered with the ATO’: https://www.ato.gov.au/Statement of Tax Record/ Not registered with ATO/International Tenderers 
Tenderers that are a new Australian business or a foreign company can apply:
using the Statement of tax record application form available here: https://www.ato.gov.au/api/public/content/10068b9b-d852-4014-97eb-4921e960cea2_37aecd00_8672_4d3f_9443_0606cf31c6dc_pdf, and following the instructions; or
by telephone, using the details provided on the Australian Taxation Office website link above.
Further information about applying for and complying with this requirement for all Tenderers is also available at  https://www.ato.gov.au/business/bus/statement-of-tax-record/.
Mandatory Minimum Content and Format Requirements
The mandatory minimum form and content requirements are:
Tenders must be written in English and all measurements must be expressed in Australian legal units of measurement;
Tenders must contain completed and detailed responses to all TRFs 1 to 16 inclusive of all attachments, substantially in the form provided in TRFs 1 to 16
Tenders must contain a completed and executed Tenderer’s deed poll substantially in the form provided with this RFT at TRF 8; 
Tenders must contain a completed Pricing Schedule substantially in the form provided with this RFT, at TRF 11);
Tenders must include all Valid and Satisfactory Statements of Tax Record relevant to the Tenderer’s entity type; and
Tenders must include all Valid and Satisfactory Statement of Tax Record for any first-tier subcontractor that it proposes, as part of its Tender, to engage to deliver goods or services with an estimated value of over $4 million (GST inclusive); and
Note to Tenderers: 
The Minimum Content and Format Requirement in clauses 1.2.1.e and 1.2.1.f of this tender Response Form 1, addresses Parts 6.a, 6.d, 6.e, 9.a, 13.a.i and 13.a.iv. of the Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy. Where the Department determines that a failure of a Tenderer to comply with this Minimum Content and Format Requirement in clause 1.2.1e and 1.2.1.f above was an unintentional error of form and the Department elects to provide the Tenderer with an opportunity to correct that error, then Part 6.j of the Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy will apply, including the requirement that any such error must be corrected within 10 Business Days of a request from the Department.

Tender Response Form (TRF) 4: Confidential Information  
Identifying Confidential Information 
Note to Tenderers: Tenderers should indicate below what information they consider should be protected as confidential information following contract award, if selected as successful, and the reasons why they consider that information is confidential.  Tenderers should understand that Commonwealth policy is that information should not be subject to an obligation of confidence unless there is a good reason to do so. Tender Response Form (TRF) 6: Compliance with Draft Contract
Note to Tenderers: The Department has a strong preference for the standard clauses of the Contract to be accepted as drafted.  If a Tender response does not raise any issues in relation to the Contract, the Department will proceed on the basis that the terms and conditions of the Contract are acceptable to that Tenderer.  Unless clearly justified, the Department does not anticipate negotiating on the standard clauses.  The Tenderer’s capacity to comply with the Contract may be assessed against the evaluation criteria, as applicable.   Note to be deleted by Tenderer
Compliance with Draft Contract
Extent to which the Tenderer can Comply
Tenderers must state their compliance or otherwise with the Contract issued with this RFT (“Statement of Compliance”).
A response, in accordance with and including any information required by the Tender Conditions, must be provided against each paragraph or clause.
If appropriate, compliance may be indicated against an entire section or groups of clauses e.g. Clauses 3.10 - 3.20 inclusive 'Complies'.
The Tenderer must respond to the Statement of Compliance using the following terminology, defined as:
Complies—In the case of a paragraph or clause that is of an informative nature only, ‘complies’ means that the paragraph or clause has been read and understood. In the case of a paragraph or clause that would impose a contractual condition or obligation, ‘complies’ means that the Tenderer makes its offer on that condition or under that obligation. In the case of a paragraph or clause that specifies a characteristic or performance to be met when providing the Requirements, ‘complies’ means that the offer is to provide the requirement as specified.
Partially Complies—This means that the contractual condition, obligation, characteristic or performance requirement imposed by the paragraph or clause can be substantially met by the offer, subject to certain qualifications. 
Does Not Comply—This means that the complete contractual condition, obligation, characteristic or performance requirement imposed by the paragraph or clause would not be met by the offer.
If the Tender states ‘Partially Complies’ or ’Does Not Comply’ to any of the clauses it must also state the reason and the extent for the partial or non-compliance, the effect on price and the risks to the Tenderer.

Tender Response Form (TRF) 10: Response to Economic Benefit to the Australian Economy
Tenderer’s Approach to Economic Benefit to the Australian Economy 
Information to be Provided and Form to be Completed by Tenderer
The Commonwealth Procurement Rules requires an assessment of the economic benefit to the Australian Economy.
Tenderer’s must provide information on the profile of the tendering entity and detail of strategies considered relevant to how their proposed supply provides economic benefit to the Australian economy.
Profile of the Tendering Entity

Strategies of the Tendering Entity 

Note to Tenderers: Responses should describe how strategies are being realised / will be realised, as opposed to describing aspirations. For example, a strategy could have already received board approval, dedicated funding, and be in the process of implementation; detail could be provided on how technology or knowledge transfer is being realised at this moment in time. The intent is to be able to consider how likely it is that economic benefits will be realised in practice.
Examples of information Tenderers might include, but are not limited to:
•	lowest price, saving the tax payer;
•	building, leasing or procuring infrastructure that supports Australian communities;
•	providing skills and training that benefits Australian communities;
•	employing workers in Australia;
•	paying taxes in Australia;
•	the environmental benefit of the proposed solution to Australia, for example, low environmental impact through energy efficient inputs such as computers, air conditioning, telephones and paper;
•	contributing to positive social outcomes in Australian communities;
•	using of indigenous businesses;
•	using SMEs in delivering goods and services, such as a subcontractor or supplier; 
•	research and development related activities and investments (including those undertaken with universities);
•	transfer of technology to Australian businesses;
•	positive effects on international competitiveness of Australian businesses (e.g. better linking to global supply chains);
•	sharing knowledge, skills and technology with SMEs; and
•	using goods and services from a business that provides services of persons with a disability.

Price Schedule 
The Price Schedule has been designed to facilitate the comparative financial evaluation of Tenders and to provide a platform for effective management of Contract prices over the period of any resultant Contract.
The information provided in the Pricing Schedule will form the basis of the agreed pricing schedule in the draft Contract between the Commonwealth and the Successful Tenderer.
Price Schedule Requirement 
Prices are to be inclusive of all things necessary and incidental to the conduct of the required services, including all reporting requirements, in accordance with relevant sections of the SOR.  
DFAT intends to contract services under any resultant Contract against the prices tendered.  Tendered prices shall include all charges necessary and incidental to the proper delivery of the specified services, less any discounts.
The tenderer must provide a breakdown of its contract Fixed Price Lump Sum as a fully costed Bill of Quantities. 
Provisional sum amount has been allowed for unexpected and unforeseeable changes as a result of the Due Diligence phase of works. This Provisional Sum amount is an upper limit Provisional sum and all claims against it must be fully validated and deemed an unforeseeable change by the PMCA/Contract administrator.
This breakdown is to be provided based on the SOR, Drawings and the BOQ table provided, noting that the drawings and SOR take precedence over the provided BOQ template.
The BOQ is attached to this RFT as an excel spreadsheet. It is to be returned in both PDF and excel formats as part of the tender submission. Numbers within the excel document must be written in in simple form to the nearest cent and not be auto-rounded.
The rates detailed in the BOQ will be used in variations to the works beyond that contemplated by the Drawings and Specifications.
The rates detailed in any variation must be a ‘cost to the Employer’ rate and must be inclusive of all direct and indirect costs (including additional costs and overheads), profits and other charges.
Day work rates must be included for key personnel and labor groups with prices to be inclusive of all profit, overheads, and other on-cost.
The BOQ has been issued as a tendering aid only, and is not a contract document. No omissions or errors in the item descriptions or quantities discovered during the delivery of the contract will result in the Contract Fixed Lump Sum contract price being adjusted. Whereas a high level care has been taken to ensure that the issued BOQ accurately represents the scope of the works, the tenderer is to make their own assessment as to the inclusiveness and accuracy of the BOQ and make amendments and additions where necessary.
Cost Savings 
The assessment of the Price Schedule will include an evaluation of the extent to which each Tenderer’s pricing and pricing structure, will:
demonstrate the capacity to deliver the requirements in a cost-effective way;
ensure that prices for meeting the requirements remain market competitive;
provide predictable charges with no unanticipated price increases over time; and
be flexible enough to adapt to operational needs, including changes arising out of re-structuring, strategic planning and changes to the requirement.

Tender Response Form (TRF) 13: Sustainable Procurement 
Tenderer’s Response Sustainable Procurement Policy
Environmental Management Practices
Australia has legislated a commitment to reduce national greenhouse gas emissions to net zero by 2050. Australia is also transitioning to a circular economy, in which products are either recycled, remanufactured, or re-used after they have served their initial purpose. The transition to a net zero and circular economy requires a shift in how we consume materials and how industry produces and delivers goods and services.
Sustainability and use of recycled content is a priority for the department and is of particular importance for this procurement.
The Tenderer should detail its environmental management practices including any Environmental Management Systems, policies and/or processes as applicable. 
Where the Tenderer has referred to any third party certifications, such as International Organization for Standardization (ISO) ISO14001:2015 Environmental Management Systems and ISO20400:2017 Sustainable Procurement, it should supply a copy of the third party certificates with its Tender.

Note to Tenderers: 
Under Australian Government procurement policy, you are obliged to indicate whether or not your organisation is covered by the Workplace Gender Equality Act 2012 (Cth) (the WGE Act). Your organisation is covered by the WGE Act if it is a ‘relevant employer’, defined as being a non-public sector employer (including higher education institutions, trade unions and not-for-profit organisations) of 100 or more employees in Australia. 
Please note that not being an Australian Business will not affect the evaluation of a company’s capability to undertake the works in this RFT. If there is any inconsistency between another part of this RFT and this checklist, the other part of this RFT will prevail.

made to check on site all built dimensions. No detailed plans are

9 
As per document “PMSB-25007-C71 
- HWC RFT - PART 2 - Scope of 
Requirements” 1.12.1.e. states 
“Removal and treatment of mouldy 
plasterboard walls and ceilings”.  
Tenderers are advised that the intent of Clause 1.12.1(e) is to 
ensure the complete removal and remediation of mould-affected 
plasterboard and the underlying cause of mould growth. 
DFAT will accept either: 
• 
Removal and replacement of the entire affected 
plasterboard sheet; or 
• 
Removal (cutting out) and replacement of the mould-
affected sections of plasterboard, 
provided the proposed approach achieves complete remediation 
of all mould-affected materials, addresses any underlying 

 
 
OFFICIAL
OFFICIAL
moisture ingress issues, and results in a compliant and fully 
restored finished surface. 
Tenderers should allow for all works necessary to achieve the 
above outcome and should include details of their proposed 
methodology within their tender submission. DFAT reserves the 
right to review and approve the final remediation approach during 
construction.

1.11
Electric Light, Power & Communications
ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION SERVICES
Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services Works shall be executed strictly in 
accordance with the electrical and communications drawings, legends, schedules, notes, 
details, and specifications forming part of the contract documents, read in conjunction with 
all other contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably inferred, whether or not individually or 
expressly detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing distribution boards
note
# OFFICIAL

 OFFICIAL#
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO 
protection and reconnection to existing distribution boards, without entitlement to additional 
payment except where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, joinery, appliance layouts, and service

inferred from the contract documents, whether 
or not individually or expressly detailed.
note

plant, equipment, coordination, testing, 
commissioning, certification, approvals, and 
incidental works necessary to deliver fully 
operational and compliant mechanical services.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades, builders’ work in connection 
with mechanical services, penetrations, 
supports, access requirements, and making 
good associated with the Works.

design intent shall be clearly identified at tender 
stage. Unidentified departures shall not 
constitute a variation.

 OFFICIAL#
ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION 
SERVICES
Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services 
Works shall be executed strictly in accordance 
with the electrical and communications 
drawings, legends, schedules, notes, details, 
and specifications forming part of the contract 
documents, read in conjunction with all other 
contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully 
examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note

 OFFICIAL#

 OFFICIAL#
ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION

Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services 
Works shall be executed strictly in accordance 
with the electrical and communications 
drawings, legends, schedules, notes, details, 
and specifications forming part of the contract 
documents, read in conjunction with all other 
contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully 
examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note

# OFFICIAL

 OFFICIAL#
ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION 
SERVICES
Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services 
Works shall be executed strictly in accordance 
with the electrical and communications 
drawings, legends, schedules, notes, details, 
and specifications forming part of the contract 
documents, read in conjunction with all other 
contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully 
examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note

 OFFICIAL#

ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION

Preamble

with the electrical and communications 
drawings, legends, schedules, notes, details, 
and specifications forming part of the contract 
documents, read in conjunction with all other 
contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully 
examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note

specifications forming part of the contract documents, read in conjunction

note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully examined the site, existing

coordinated, and compliant execution of the Hydraulic Services Works, 
including all matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably inferred, 
whether or not expressly detailed.
note
The Works shall include the complete provision of all labour, materials, 
plant, equipment, testing, inspections, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to deliver fully functional hydraulic 
services, including water services, sanitary plumbing, drainage, stormwater, 
hot water systems, valves, fixtures, fittings, accessories, and connections to 
existing services.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all works associated with existing services, 
including investigation, CCTV inspection, testing, modification, alteration, 
temporary diversion, isolation, removal, and sealing of redundant services, 
without entitlement to additional payment, except where expressly directed 
as a variation under the contract.
note

and the builder, including builders’ work in connection with hydraulic 
services, penetrations, sleeves, fire

panels, temporary services, staging requirements, and reinstatement of

8.11
Electric Light, Power & Communications
ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION SERVICES
Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services Works shall be executed 
strictly in accordance with the electrical and communications drawings, 
legends, schedules, notes, details, and specifications forming part of the 
contract documents, read in conjunction with all other contract 
documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully examined the existing 
electrical and communications installations, distribution boards, submains, 
cabling routes, and all documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, and safe execution of the 
Electrical and Communications Services Works, including all matters 
shown, specified, noted, or reasonably inferred, whether or not individually 
or expressly detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, the full provision of labour, 
materials, equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, approvals, and 
making good necessary to deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, conduits, and future-proofing 
pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, assessment, isolation, 
disconnection, modification, retention, replacement, or removal of existing 
electrical and communications services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection and reconnection to 
existing distribution boards, without entitlement to additional payment 
except where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination with other trades and 
systems, including mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and structural 
works. No claim for additional payment or time shall arise due to 
coordination issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall congestion, or 
clashes that could reasonably have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, switches, luminaires, 
communications points, and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated on site to suit building 
elements, furniture, joinery, appliance layouts, and service clearances, 
while maintaining compliance with AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all penetrations, sleeves, fire-rated and 
acoustic sealing, access panels, builders’ work in connection with electrical 
and communications services, and reinstatement of affected surfaces 
necessary to deliver a complete installation.
note
The Contractor shall be solely responsible for testing, commissioning, 
certification, inspection coordination, and provision of all compliance 
documentation, test reports, as
‑
built drawings, and operation and 
maintenance manuals for the Electrical and Communications Services.
note
Any assumptions, exclusions, substitutions, qualifications, or departures 
from the documented scope, systems, performance requirements, or 
coordination obligations shall be clearly identified in writing at tender stage. 
Any matter not so identified shall be deemed included and shall not 
constitute grounds for a variation.
note

note

certification, approvals, and making good

note

9.11
Electric Light, Power & Communications
# OFFICIAL

 OFFICIAL#
ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION 
SERVICES
Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services 
Works shall be executed strictly in accordance 
with the electrical and communications 
drawings, legends, schedules, notes, details, 
and specifications forming part of the contract 
documents, read in conjunction with all other 
contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully 
examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note
# OFFICIAL

 OFFICIAL#
The Contractor shall allow for all penetrations, 
sleeves, fire-rated and acoustic sealing, access 
panels, builders’ work in connection with 
electrical and communications services, and 
reinstatement of affected surfaces necessary to 
deliver a complete installation.
note
The Contractor shall be solely responsible for 
testing, commissioning, certification, inspection 
coordination, and provision of all compliance 
documentation, test reports, as
‑
built drawings, 
and operation and maintenance manuals for the 
Electrical and Communications Services.
note
Any assumptions, exclusions, substitutions, 
qualifications, or departures from the 
documented scope, systems, performance 
requirements, or coordination obligations shall 
be clearly identified in writing at tender stage. 
Any matter not so identified shall be deemed 
included and shall not constitute grounds for a 
variation.
note

12.9
Mechanical service
MECHANICAL SERVICES
Preambles
The Mechanical Services Works shall be carried 
out in accordance with the mechanical services 
drawings, schedules, details, and specifications 
forming part of the contract documents.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have 
examined and allowed for the complete scope 
of the Mechanical Services Works, including all 
work shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred from the contract documents, whether 
or not individually or expressly detailed.
note
The Works shall include all labour, materials, 
plant, equipment, coordination, testing, 
commissioning, certification, approvals, and 
incidental works necessary to deliver fully 
operational and compliant mechanical services.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades, builders’ work in connection 
with mechanical services, penetrations, 
supports, access requirements, and making 
good associated with the Works.
note
Any departure from the documented scope or 
design intent shall be clearly identified at tender 
stage. Unidentified departures shall not 
constitute a variation.
note

note

documentation and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, and compliant execution 
of the Hydraulic Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not expressly detailed.
note
The Works shall include the complete provision 
of all labour, materials, plant, equipment, 
testing, inspections, commissioning, 
certification, approvals, and making good 
necessary to deliver fully functional hydraulic 
services, including water services, sanitary 
plumbing, drainage, stormwater, hot water 
systems, valves, fixtures, fittings, accessories, 
and connections to existing services.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all works 
associated with existing services, including 
investigation, CCTV inspection, testing, 
modification, alteration, temporary diversion, 
isolation, removal, and sealing of redundant 
services, without entitlement to additional 
payment, except where expressly directed as a 
variation under the contract.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades, authorities, and the builder, 
including builders’ work in connection with 
hydraulic services, penetrations, sleeves, 
fire
‑
rated and acoustic sealing, access panels, 
temporary services, staging requirements, and 
reinstatement of affected building elements.
note
Any proposed exclusions, assumptions, 
qualifications, or departures from the 
documented scope or performance 
requirements shall be clearly identified in writing 
at tender stage. Any matter not so identified 
shall be deemed to be included and shall not 
constitute grounds for a variation.

# OFFICIAL

 OFFICIAL#
ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION 
SERVICES
Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services 
Works shall be executed strictly in accordance 
with the electrical and communications 
drawings, legends, schedules, notes, details, 
and specifications forming part of the contract 
documents, read in conjunction with all other 
contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully 
examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note
# OFFICIAL

 OFFICIAL#

sleeves, fire-rated and acoustic sealing, access 
panels, builders’ work in connection with

other contract documents.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully

documentation and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, and compliant execution 
of the Hydraulic Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not expressly detailed.
note
The Works shall include the complete provision 
of all labour, materials, plant, equipment, 
testing, inspections, commissioning, 
certification, approvals, and making good 
necessary to deliver fully functional hydraulic 
services, including water services, sanitary 
plumbing, drainage, stormwater, hot water 
systems, valves, fixtures, fittings, accessories, 
and connections to existing services.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all works 
associated with existing services, including 
investigation, CCTV inspection, testing, 
modification, alteration, temporary diversion, 
isolation, removal, and sealing of redundant 
services, without entitlement to additional 
payment, except where expressly directed as a 
variation under the contract.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades, authorities, and the builder, 
including builders’ work in connection with 
hydraulic services, penetrations, sleeves, 
fire
‑
rated and acoustic sealing, access panels, 
temporary services, staging requirements, and 
reinstatement of affected building elements.
note
Any proposed exclusions, assumptions, 
qualifications, or departures from the 
documented scope or performance 
requirements shall be clearly identified in writing 
at tender stage. Any matter not so identified 
shall be deemed to be included and shall not 
constitute grounds for a variation.

# OFFICIAL

 OFFICIAL#
ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION 
SERVICES
Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services 
Works shall be executed strictly in accordance 
with the electrical and communications 
drawings, legends, schedules, notes, details, 
and specifications forming part of the contract 
documents, read in conjunction with all other 
contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully 
examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note
# OFFICIAL

 OFFICIAL#
The Contractor shall allow for all penetrations, 
sleeves, fire-rated and acoustic sealing, access 
panels, builders’ work in connection with 
electrical and communications services, and 
reinstatement of affected surfaces necessary to 
deliver a complete installation.
note

testing, commissioning, certification, inspection

‑
built drawings,

note

ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION

Preamble
The Electrical and Communications Services 
Works shall be executed strictly in accordance 
with the electrical and communications 
drawings, legends, schedules, notes, details, 
and specifications forming part of the contract 
documents, read in conjunction with all other 
contract documentation.
note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully 
examined the existing electrical and 
communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note

ELECTRICAL AND COMMUNICATION

note
The Contractor shall be deemed to have fully

communications installations, distribution 
boards, submains, cabling routes, and all 
documentation, and to have allowed for the 
complete, coordinated, integrated, compliant, 
and safe execution of the Electrical and 
Communications Services Works, including all 
matters shown, specified, noted, or reasonably 
inferred, whether or not individually or expressly 
detailed.
note
The Works shall include, but not be limited to, 
the full provision of labour, materials, 
equipment, testing, commissioning, certification, 
approvals, and making good necessary to 
deliver fully operational systems, including:
- general and equipment power;
- lighting and lighting controls;
- ceiling fans, exhaust fan interlocks, and 
appliance connections;
- smoke detection and fire-related electrical 
services;
- electrical isolation, disconnection, demolition, 
and reconnection works;
- communications, data, TV, router outlets, 
conduits, and future-proofing pathways
- surge protection devices;
- upgrades and modifications within existing 
distribution boards.
note
The Contractor shall allow for the investigation, 
assessment, isolation, disconnection, 
modification, retention, replacement, or removal 
of existing electrical and communications 
services as required to complete the Works, 
including rewiring to new RCD/RCBO protection 
and reconnection to existing distribution boards, 
without entitlement to additional payment except 
where expressly directed as a variation.
note
The Contractor shall allow for all coordination 
with other trades and systems, including 
mechanical, hydraulic, fire, architectural, and 
structural works. No claim for additional 
payment or time shall arise due to coordination 
issues, access constraints, ceiling or wall 
congestion, or clashes that could reasonably 
have been foreseen.
note
The location, heights, and routing of outlets, 
switches, luminaires, communications points, 
and equipment shown on the drawings are 
indicative. Final locations shall be coordinated 
on site to suit building elements, furniture, 
joinery, appliance layouts, and service 
clearances, while maintaining compliance with 
AS/NZS 3000, AS/CA S009, and other 
applicable standards.
note

OFFICIAL 
OFFICIAL 
ADDENDUM 03 PMSB 25007 – C71 
to the Request for Tender PMSB-25007-C71- Provision of Head Works Contractor – Konedobu, Port 
Moresby, Papua New Guinea released on 26/06/2026 
DFAT provides the following information in accordance with the Standard Conditions of Tender.  
 
No. 
Question/Clarification 
Response 
1 
Should tenderers allow for a 
Provisional Sum or contingency 
within the Tender Price to cover 
potential latent conditions, or 
will such works be treated as 
Contract Variations if 
encountered? 
The Contract contains provisions dealing with latent conditions and 
the Due Diligence Phase. Any entitlement to additional time or cost 
arising from latent or concealed conditions will be assessed in 
accordance with the terms of the Contract and the particular 
circumstances encountered. 
2 
Please confirm whether the site 
will be available for work on 
weekends, including Saturdays. 
Access to site will be negotiated with the preferred tenderers. No 
works will be undertaken on the weekends or public holidays 
without written permission from DFAT 
3 
Several sections of the 
document Part 1 - Terms and 
Conditions contain the notation 
"Error! Reference source not 
found." Provide clarification for 
the relevant sections. 
Current Text 
Correct Reference 
The Conditions for Participation 
are at clause Error! Reference 
source not found. of Tender 
Response Form (TRF) 1. 
The Conditions for 
Participation are at 
Clause 1.1.1. of Tender 
Response Form (TRF) 1. 
The Minimum Content and 
Format Requirements are at 
clause Error! Reference source 
not found. of Tender Response 
Form (TRF) 1 
The Minimum Content 
and Format Requirements 
are at Clause 1.2.1. of 
Tender Response Form 
(TRF) 1 
The Successful Tenderer will be 
required to hold and maintain 
insurance arrangements as 
required in clause Error! 
Reference source not found. of 
TRF 5 and in accordance with 
the terms and conditions of the 
Contract. 
The Successful Tenderer 
will be required to hold 
and maintain insurance 
arrangements as required 
in Clause 5.2. of TRF 5 
and in accordance with 
the terms and conditions 
of the Contract. 
4 
Section 1.6.1(c)(i) of the 
Responsibilities states that the 
Contractor is responsible for the 
submission of documentation 
required for building permits 
and local authority approvals. 
Please clarify the intended 
allocation of responsibilities. 
The Contractor is responsible for identifying all approvals, permits, 
licences, certifications and other regulatory requirements necessary 
to undertake and complete the Works, and for preparing, 
coordinating, submitting and managing all associated 
documentation and approval processes with the relevant authorities. 

 
 
OFFICIAL 
OFFICIAL 
5 
Given the anticipated Contract 
duration of approximately 4.5 
years, please confirm whether 
Tenderers are expected to 
include allowances for: 
• 
Material cost 
escalation; 
• 
Labour cost escalation; 
• 
Fuel price increases; 
• 
Freight and shipping 
cost increases; and 
• 
Other inflationary 
impacts. 
Tenderers are to submit a lump sum price for the Works, broken 
down in the form and structure set out in the Pricing Schedule 
Tender Response Form in the RFT. 
As provided in clause 5.1 of the Formal Instrument of Agreement of 
the Draft Contract, the Contract Sum is not subject to adjustment for 
rise or fall in the cost of labour, materials or services, fluctuations in 
import duty or exchange rates, additional sales taxes or other taxes 
or imposts, or for any other matter or thing whatsoever affecting the 
cost to the Contractor of carrying out and completing the Works, 
except as expressly provided in the Draft Contract. 
The limited circumstances in which the Contract Sum may be 
adjusted are set out in the Draft Contract and include: 
• 
changes in Legislative Requirements coming into force after the 
Date of the Contract that could not reasonably have been 
anticipated and that cause the Contractor to incur more or less 
cost (clause 11.2); 
• 
Latent Conditions encountered during carrying out the Works 
that cause the Contractor to carry out more work or incur more 
cost than could reasonably have been anticipated at the 
relevant Date of Delivery Phase Approval, limited to latent 
structural defects in the existing buildings at each Site (clause 
25.3); and 
• 
adjustments to the Provisional Allowance (Cost) for during the 
Due Diligence Phase of each Stage (clause 6.5). 
Tenderers remain solely responsible for determining the adequacy 
of their pricing assumptions and risk allowances.
